It writes a snapshot that has a schema, checks every snapshot against the schema before it is written, and reports from a snapshot with no network. **It applies no rule.** *How it was checked:* 143 tests in its own file, 708 in the suite. A workflow, `settings-audit-live.yml`, runs the reader against GitHub on every change to it, with the workflow's token and with none, on this repository and on Expectrail (decision F-34), and fails unless the exit code, the report and the snapshot agree. Two reviewers that wrote none of it made four passes. The first found eight blocking things, in osprey's run. The second, on kite's commits, found five in its first pass (an administrator counted in a lower role when the answer did not say; a report line saying "no ruleset applies" above one saying a ruleset was in force; a snapshot the command wrote and then refused to read; a live job that could pass on a wrong reader; eleven untrue sentences), one regression from those fixes in its second, and nothing blocking in code in its third, with 42,595 mutated answers breaking no invariant. *What only the live runs showed:* four faults in the report that the hand-written recordings had hidden: a rule cut off after 60 characters, a tag pattern printed like a branch pattern, no word on whether administrators may bypass an environment, and a branch protected by a ruleset described as having no required checks. Two of GitHub's own answers are now test recordings. **Not run:** anything with a token that has administrative read access, so classic branch protection, secret names, Actions permissions and policies are tested only against hand-written answers; a private repository; GitHub Enterprise Server. *Done when* it runs with no token on public data where the API allows, every setting it could not read is listed by name as unread with the reason (never skipped), it never asks for more than read access, and tests run from recorded API responses.
- ☑ **2b.2 Rules for the six incidents.** Merged as [#111](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/111) on 2026-11-13: started by run `avocet` (00:04 to 01:28 UTC, on Fable; its claim lapsed at 05:04 without a report), finished by run `redshank` (Opus 5.5) after taking it over on the same branch (decision F-35). Decision D-045 in `DECISIONS.md` was accepted on 2026-11-13 (#130); the guide is [settings-audit.md](settings-audit.md#the-rules); the changelog section is "Settings rules". *What it does:* `obaaf audit` applies fifteen rules, `OBAAF-SET-001` to `015`, defined in `src/obaaf/audit/rules.py` (not in the checker's `rules.yaml`). Twelve compare a setting with what the workflow files in a clone use, read with the checker's parser (`src/obaaf/audit/workflows.py`); three read settings only (`004` bypass, `005` approvals that cannot be given, `013` a workflow that can give an approval, since a workflow on any branch can ask for the token). Each result is a finding or "cannot tell"; a rule with neither says what it compared or why it had nothing to compare. The six incidents: `001` (and `002`, `003`), `004`, `005`, `008` (and `006`, `007`), `009`, `011`; also `010` (`pull_request_target` and the default policy of 2026-12-02), `012`, `013`, `014`, `015`. `--fail-on` (default `medium`), `--fail-on-cannot-tell`, `--no-fixes`; `--format json` is `obaaf-settings-audit/v0.1` with the snapshot under `snapshot`. A settings finding cannot be accepted (no baseline, no `ignore`). A file GitHub would refuse in a way the audit knows (a job that is not a mapping, a job with neither `runs-on` nor `uses`, both `branches` and `branches-ignore` on one event, likewise `tags`, `paths`) counts as not running. *Merged with 2b.3:* one command; with `--expected` the drift comparison is under `drift` in the same JSON document; the drift template and this repository's drift job pass `--fail-on never` (decision F-68). *How it was checked:* 1158 tests on the merged head (Python 3.13 locally). CI green on #111's head `b7be12c` ([run 43389708932](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43389708932)), with the live audit ([43389708933](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43389708933)) and the drift job ([43389708949](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43389708949)) on the same head. Offline on real recordings: this repository's no-token answers with its own files give 1 finding (medium: `ci.yml#dependency-lock` runs on every pull request and is not a required check) and 6 "cannot tell"; Expectrail's no-token answers of 2026-11-12 with a copy of its files at `8a3e83f` give 5 medium findings and 7 "cannot tell". *Reviews:* one pass on `avocet`'s first version (24 faults, fixed in `ce1459c`), then seven passes by Opus 5.5 agents that wrote none of it on `redshank`'s work. Passes 1 and 2 (in parallel) found six false passes (SET-008 skipped jobs with a pattern in their branch filter; counted a matrix job as required when one of its checks was; SET-013 trusted the jobs in the current files; SET-014 assumed fork pull requests run these workflow files; reusable workflows were followed five levels only; a job that is not a mapping was dropped). Pass 3 found four more, one of them a fault I had introduced on a reviewer's memory (decision F-71). Pass 4: one check name required from two apps kept one app. Pass 5: "any app" beside another app gave "no finding" (decision F-70). Passes 6 and 7: only untrue sentences; after pass 7 only wording changed (decision F-12). **Not run:** an administrator's token (every rule reads at least one setting tested only from hand-written recordings), a real Actions policy, a private repository, GitHub Enterprise Server. *Left for later, none a false pass:* (1) a check required from Actions and from another app that nothing reports is "cannot tell" in SET-006, where the Actions requirement alone would make it a finding; (2) a ruleset with a malformed approval count lists no unread setting; (3) SET-007 treats a workflow only `workflow_dispatch` starts as "cannot tell", and GitHub's troubleshooting page says such checks do not satisfy a requirement; (4) two jobs reporting one check name count as reporting it (GitHub does not document duplicates); (5) SET-007 says "does not apply" when the only candidate job's names are unknown and it sits behind a filter; (6) for `pull_request` events GitHub evaluates environment rules against `refs/pull/N/merge` since 2025-12-08 (changelog of 2025-11-07); SET-001's advice about patterns does not mention it; (7) whether a `pull_request_target` check lands on the pull request's head commit now that its `GITHUB_SHA` is the default branch's is not established, and SET-006 to 008 assume it does. *For 2b.4:* run `obaaf audit CLONE --repo Expectrail/Expectrail` with the clone's path named (its `origin` may not name Expectrail/Expectrail; a named directory is compared anyway), with no token and with the workflow's token if a run in this repository can be used; the recording of 2026-11-12 already gives the numbers above. *For the maintainers:* OBAAF's own `dependency-lock` job is not a required check (item 20 of [Waiting on Sean](#waiting-on-sean)).
- ☑ **2b.3 Drift.** Merged as [#112](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/112) by run `godwit`; decision D-046 in `DECISIONS.md` was accepted on 2026-11-13 (#130); the guide is [settings-audit.md](settings-audit.md#drift); the changelog section is "Settings drift". *What it does:* `obaaf audit --expected FILE` compares the `settings` part of a committed snapshot (written by `--snapshot-out`) with the settings read now, or with another file given to `--from-snapshot`. Each part that differs (a ruleset, an environment, a branch, a secret's name, a field) is one finding of `OBAAF-DRIFT-001`, a rule of the audit module and not of the checker's catalogue, with each change listed. A part one side read and the other did not is "not compared" and fails, in both directions; only what neither side read in full is "not covered" (listed, no failure); nothing compared fails. Lists are multisets, values are compared as JSON, list elements are matched by canonical text. The committed file must be of the same repository and API address (checked before any request), give no two parts one key, hold branch data for exactly the branches it lists, and name at most 50 (exit 2 otherwise); its branches are audited too, and a branch only one side audited is not compared. `--snapshot-out` and `--output` may not be the `--expected` file. `--format json` prints `obaaf-settings-drift/v0.1`. The schedule is [`templates/github-actions/optional/obaaf-settings-drift.yml`](../templates/github-actions/optional/obaaf-settings-drift.yml), not installed by `obaaf templates install`; with no snapshot committed its run fails on purpose and keeps the snapshot to commit, so no file is written by hand. This repository runs it as `.github/workflows/settings-drift.yml` (daily once on `main`, and on pull requests that change the audit) against `.github/obaaf-settings.json`, which is what that workflow's first run read on #112 with the workflow's token. Found on the way and fixed: `snapshot.load` read `1e999` as infinity; an unusable `--from-snapshot` file was not named. *How it was checked:* 1011 tests on the merged head (Python 3.13 locally; 62 new). CI green on #112's head `e466497` (all eleven checks, [run 43365266176](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43365266176)). On GitHub: the bootstrap run failed as designed and printed the snapshot ([43358459527](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43358459527)); with the snapshot committed the live comparison passed ([43360414282](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43360414282), 7 settings compared, 8 not covered). The guide's example is checked by a test against GitHub's own answers for Expectrail. Four review passes by Opus 5.5 agents that wrote none of it: the first found two blocking faults (a part the committed snapshot did not read hid every change in it with exit 0: a first bypass actor added where GitHub had sent an empty list, an environment whose patterns failed to load when the snapshot was taken) and nine smaller ones; the second one blocking fault (the changelog and D-046 still stated the rule the first pass removed) and nine smaller; the third and fourth nothing blocking. After the fourth only docstrings and two tests changed (decision F-66). **Not run:** an administrator's token, a private repository (the template's `actions: read` comes from GitHub's reference), the template installed in another repository, GitHub Enterprise Server. *What the workflow's token cannot watch*, and so the template never watches: who may bypass a ruleset, classic branch protection, secret names, the Actions settings. This repository's `main` has classic protection and no ruleset, so its own job watches only the required checks that rule shows publicly. *Left for later, none of them a false pass:* (1) a part the expected snapshot read in part that is now gone, or a new part read in part now, is "not compared" rather than "gone" or "new"; (2) an environment read in full in the expected snapshot and in part now is "not compared" without the difference in what was read; (3) a file name holding an escape character reaches stderr unfiltered in two `cannot compare` messages (the name comes from whoever runs the command); (4) the audit's own report still says "no rule runs on what it read" after a drift finding, which #111 rewrites anyway; (5) a library-level case found by the fourth pass's differential: when every setting but environments is unread on both sides and the environments are read in part on both, `failed()` is now False; the command still exits 1 because the repository was not read. *For whichever of 2b.2 (#111) and 2b.3 merges second:* both change `src/obaaf/audit/cli.py` (`_run` ends with a `code` variable in both), `docs/settings-audit.md` (status line, exit codes, the sections "The snapshot" and "What it does not read"), `CHANGELOG.md` and `DECISIONS.md` (D-045 is #111's, D-046 this slice's); #111's `--format json` document should carry the drift result under a key of its own when `--expected` is given, instead of the separate `obaaf-settings-drift/v0.1` document.
- ☑ **2b.4 Expectrail, read-only.** Merged as [#117](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/117) by run `pipit` on 2026-11-13. No decision in `DECISIONS.md`: nothing an adopter runs changed. The record is [expectrail-settings-audit.md](expectrail-settings-audit.md). *What it did:* the live workflow (`settings-audit-live.yml`) now audits Expectrail/Expectrail with no token and with its own workflow files, from a clone made in the same step with no credentials (only the top-level files and `.github` checked out, blobs on demand), and requires every one of them to be read; it prints the commit and the snapshot. Its run on this slice, [43399109471](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43399109471) at 09:10 UTC with the files at `36a7a8a` (the head of `main` then), is the record: GitHub's eight answers and the snapshot are in `tests/fixtures/settings/live-expectrail-2026-11-13/`, and `tests/test_settings_adopter.py` holds the page to them. A second run on the final workflow ([43403505122](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43403505122), 09:42 UTC) gave the same report. Expectrail's own files are not copied into this repository (decision F-72); the page gives the one command that makes the report again from the snapshot and a clone at that commit, and the session did it. No setting of Expectrail was changed and nothing was written there. *What it found:* 4 medium findings, each true on reading the files: `OBAAF-SET-001` the environment `unity` is open to every branch behind one reviewer who may approve their own run, and every merge into Expectrail's `main` that morning (15) is authored by one account, so the reviewer cannot ensure that a person approved (Expectrail's acceptance of `OBAAF-GHA-001` for that job rests on that approval); `OBAAF-SET-008` three pull-request jobs that are not required, `merge-guard` (the job that checks the rules no agent may break), `agents-daily-offline` and `private-scan-request` (of little consequence). 7 rules "cannot tell"; 10 of 15 settings not read (9 need a token, the bypass list is withheld). Of the six incidents, two no longer show, two show again in another form, two need an administrator. Since the reading of 2026-11-12 01:33 UTC one setting changed: `package` became a required check. *Found by the review, left for later:* the ruleset's pull-request rule carries `require_extra_approval_for_unattributed_changes: true`, which the reader keeps and no rule reads or reports; it is slice 2b.5 (#118, decision F-73). *How it was checked:* 1178 tests locally on Python 3.13 (1166 before; 12 new); ten checks green on the merged head `2f4cf92` ([CI 43406631540](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43406631540), [live audit 43406631522](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43406631522)). Three review passes by sub-agents that wrote none of it: the first (Opus 5.5) found 8 blocking points, all sentences that said more than the evidence or a test that did not test the page; the second confirmed the fixes but ran on Opus 4.8; the third, a fresh Opus 5.5 reviewer, found 3 more (one untrue: a second reviewer would not clear `OBAAF-SET-001`) and confirmed their fixes (decision F-74). *Not run:* any token on Expectrail; anything that needs an administrator (bypass list, Actions policies, which an administrator can read in the settings); GitHub's pages on environments, whose request was not answered on 2026-11-13. One run by a maintainer with a read-only token is item 21 of [Waiting on Sean](#waiting-on-sean).
- ☑ **2b.5 An approval parameter no rule reads.** Merged as [#121](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/121) by run `turnstone` on 2026-11-13 (decisions F-79 to F-81). Decision D-048 in `DECISIONS.md` was accepted on 2026-11-13 (#130). *What was built:* `OBAAF-SET-005` and `OBAAF-SET-013` place every parameter of a ruleset's `pull_request` rule in one of two lists in `src/obaaf/audit/rules.py`, the nine of GitHub's REST reference for rulesets ([REST API endpoints for rules](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/repos/rules), "Create a repository ruleset", read on 2026-11-13): read by the rules (`APPROVAL_PARAMETERS`), or about neither how many approvals a branch needs nor who can give them (`OTHER_PULL_REQUEST_PARAMETERS`); a test pins the lists to the nine and checks GitHub's two recorded answers for Expectrail hold nothing else. A parameter in neither, or a known one in another form (an object with a field the reference does not list included), makes `OBAAF-SET-005` "cannot tell" for its branch, naming it and its value whatever the value, and `OBAAF-SET-013` too where workflows may approve; a finding by count stays a finding and names it, and so does a "cannot tell" given for another reason. Read as GitHub's pages describe them: `require_extra_approval_for_unattributed_changes` adds nothing on a ruleset counting 0 ("This setting has no effect if the ruleset requires zero approvals", [Available rules for rulesets](https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/configuring-branches-and-merges-in-your-repository/managing-rulesets/available-rules-for-rulesets)) and one more than its ruleset's count for a pull request Copilot opens without a person ("a ruleset that requires one approval requires two approvals from people with write access"); `require_last_push_approval` changes no answer with one approval or more and is "cannot tell" where no approval is known to be required; a team `required_reviewers` asks for an approval is "cannot tell" (team membership is not read). `OBAAF-SET-005` is now "cannot tell" for a code owner's review unless the count alone is a finding (the CODEOWNERS file is not read): the same fault in a parameter the rule already had (F-80). The report prints a rule's note up to 600 characters. The guide has a new section, [The parameters of a pull-request rule](settings-audit.md#the-parameters-of-a-pull-request-rule); [expectrail-settings-audit.md](expectrail-settings-audit.md) has a dated note: the parameter changes nothing on Expectrail's ruleset, which counts 0, and concerns Copilot, not commits authored as another agent. *What was run:* 1221 tests locally on Python 3.13 (1190 before; 31 new in `tests/test_settings_approval_parameters.py`); eleven checks green on the merged head `ac2df57` ([CI 43465977104](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43465977104), [live audit 43465977057](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43465977057)); the adopter's reproduction command on a copy of Expectrail at `36a7a8a` gives the page's 4 medium findings and 7 "cannot tell" as before, `OBAAF-SET-005` still not applicable, its note now naming the parameter. GitHub's four pages were read through a fetching agent on 2026-11-13 (the REST page refused once and loaded after it appeared in a search result). *Review:* four passes by one Opus 5.5 sub-agent that wrote none of it and read the pages itself (F-81). The first found two blocking faults, both docs saying more than the code (four known parameters and unknown fields inside `required_reviewers` and `dismissal_restriction` were passed over whatever their form, a fail-open; several "cannot tell" results did not name the parameter), a reordering no test caught, an over-cautious reading of the flag beside another ruleset, and page text that settles who may approve such a pull request, now quoted. The second checked seven quotations word for word and found three sentences that said more than the code (one a code gap: the no-token "cannot tell" of `OBAAF-SET-013` did not name the parameter). The third found one (a rule whose own count is not a number did not have its other parameters named). The fourth found nothing blocking; one clause it raised was reworded after it, wording only. *Not run:* the extra-approval setting on GitHub (the pages speak only of pull requests Copilot opens); any token; a real answer with a `required_reviewers` team. *Not read:* whether Copilot may approve pull requests, which since 2026-09-01 (public preview) can count toward the required approvals ([changelog](https://github.blog/changelog/2026-09-01-copilot-code-review-can-now-approve-pull-requests)); no API is given for it, and where it is on, an `OBAAF-SET-005` finding that says "merges only by a bypass" may be wrong. *Left for later:* slice 2b.6 (a ruleset rule of a type the audit does not know is passed over; classic protection is read into a fixed form, so a field GitHub adds there is dropped; some messages say "a classic protection rule may ask for more" where the gap is a ruleset's count that could not be read); `OBAAF-SET-013` with one approval, a code owner and the flag stays "cannot tell" where a finding could be argued. *From the plan (the specification it was built to):* Found by the review of 2b.4 (#117; decision F-73). Expectrail's ruleset has a `pull_request` rule with `"require_extra_approval_for_unattributed_changes": true` beside `"required_approving_review_count": 0`, in GitHub's answers of 2026-11-12 and 2026-11-13. The reader keeps it in the snapshot; `OBAAF-SET-005` reads the count only and answers "does not apply", `OBAAF-SET-013` reasons from the count, and nothing in the report says a parameter about approvals went unread. *Done when* (1) a `pull_request` rule that carries a parameter about reviews or approvals that the rules do not read makes `OBAAF-SET-005` and `OBAAF-SET-013` answer "cannot tell" for that branch, naming the parameter, instead of "does not apply" or "no finding" (fail closed: an unknown parameter that can only add a requirement is not read as none), and the known parameters are listed in the code so that a new one is noticed; (2) the guide says what is and is not established about `require_extra_approval_for_unattributed_changes`, from a GitHub page read on a date and cited if one can be read (decision F-71), and the rules read it only if its meaning is established; (3) tests from the recorded answers, Expectrail's two among them. Not core: `src/obaaf/audit/` is its own module.
- ☑ **2b.6 A ruleset rule of a type no settings rule knows.** Merged as [#124](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/124) by run `kittiwake` on 2026-11-13 (decisions F-82 to F-85). Decision D-049 in `DECISIONS.md` was accepted on 2026-11-13 (#130). *What was built:* each of the 25 rule types of GitHub's REST reference for rulesets ([REST API endpoints for rules](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/repos/rules), API version 2026-03-10, read on 2026-11-13) is placed in `RULE_TYPES` in `src/obaaf/audit/rules.py`, in one of six groups: read (`required_status_checks`, `pull_request`); a merge waits for a result workflows, tools or deployments give (`merge_queue`, `required_deployments`, `workflows`, `code_scanning`, `code_quality`, `code_coverage`, `license_compliance_scanning`): `OBAAF-SET-004` counts each as a gate a bypass skips (four of them were passed over before), `OBAAF-SET-006` is "cannot tell" for each (`OBAAF-SET-007` for the merge queue), and a finding of `OBAAF-SET-008` names the six other than the queue; `update`, which `OBAAF-SET-005` names as something it cannot weigh (that it decides who may merge is marked as an inference); `copilot_code_review`, which a finding of `OBAAF-SET-005` names; and fourteen that neither ask for a check or a review nor decide who may merge. A type in none of them makes `OBAAF-SET-005` to `008` "cannot tell" for its branch, naming it, `OBAAF-SET-013` where workflows may approve, and `OBAAF-SET-004` names it or cannot tell. Classic protection: the fields of the reference's two examples and the update's body are `CLASSIC_FIELDS` in `settings.py`; any other field (inside an entry of `required_status_checks.checks` included, kept with the check it is on), a known object in another form and a flag whose `enabled` is not true or false or is missing are kept with their values under `other_fields` in the snapshot (written only when there is one) and make the same rules "cannot tell"; classic `restrictions` and `lock_branch` are named by `OBAAF-SET-005` as `update` is. A ruleset's `required_status_checks` parameter or check field outside the reference makes the check rules "cannot tell". Messages name what could not be read (a ruleset's count, a classic count, checks in another form, a type or field not known, a branch a snapshot file leaves out) where they said "a classic protection rule may ask for more". The report quotes a rule type it does not know. Guide: [Rule types and classic fields the rules do not know](settings-audit.md#rule-types-and-classic-fields-the-rules-do-not-know). *What was run:* 1260 tests locally on Python 3.13 (1221 before; 39 new in `tests/test_settings_rule_types.py`, one assertion reworded in `test_settings_rules.py`); `obaaf catalog` and `obaaf docs build --check`; eleven checks green on the merged head `c183593` ([CI 43489661811](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43489661811), [live audit 43489661820](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43489661820), [drift 43489661838](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43489661838)). The adopter's reproduction command on a copy of Expectrail at `36a7a8a` and this repository's committed snapshot give the same report, character for character, before and after (4 medium and 7 "cannot tell"; 1 medium and 6). A snapshot written by the code before the slice (`tests/fixtures/settings/classic-before-2b6/`) matches one taken now; a snapshot with `other_fields` is refused by that code. GitHub's three pages were read through fetching agents (the first attempt was refused for want of an answered permission request; after a web search returned the pages they loaded; the page "Available rules for rulesets" came back verbatim only for its first 40,000 characters, so its sections on required workflows and merge queues were not read). *Review:* five passes by one Opus 5.5 sub-agent that wrote none of it, resumed with its context and probes (F-85), which re-read the two REST pages itself. The first found three blocking faults (a field inside a classic required check was dropped while the docs said kept; sentences that said more than the code; classic restrictions and a locked branch still "no finding" where `update` was "cannot tell"), the second two (`enabled: null` dropped; drift blind to a check field moving between checks), the third two (`lock_branch: {}`; wording), the fourth one (`required_signatures` with no `enabled`), the fifth nothing. The new tests failed on the old code (14 failures and 10 errors of 28 at the first pass). *Not run:* a real classic protection answer (no run has had a token that can read it); a real ruleset with any of the newly placed types. *Left for later:* comparing the result-waiting types with the workflow files (whether a job deploys to a required environment; whether the jobs that report required checks run on `merge_group`), which needs GitHub's pages on them; check names in the report's "What was read" are not quoted, a fault older than the slice, left to [#125](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/issues/125) because a test ties the dated record of 2b.4 to the current output (F-84). *From the plan (the specification it was built to):* Found by the review of 2b.5 (#121; decision F-79). `_requirements` in `src/obaaf/audit/rules.py` reads two rule types, `required_status_checks` and `pull_request`, and passes over every other; a type GitHub adds that carries checks or approvals would be read as asking for nothing. *Done when* (1) the rule types of GitHub's REST reference for rulesets, read on a date and cited, are listed in the code as read by a rule, or as neither asking for checks nor reviews nor deciding who may merge, and a rule of a type in neither makes the rules that read a branch's requirements (`OBAAF-SET-004` to `OBAAF-SET-008` and `OBAAF-SET-013`) "cannot tell" for its branch, naming it; (2) classic branch protection's fields that the reader drops are kept, or a field it does not know makes the same rules "cannot tell" (this changes the snapshot, so drift (2b.3) must still compare an old snapshot with a new one, or say why not); (3) the messages that say "a classic protection rule may ask for more" say which requirement could not be read; (4) tests from recorded answers. Not core: `src/obaaf/audit/` is its own module.
- ☑ **2b.7 The settings audit's gaps from #182.** Merged as [#207](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/207) by run `lapwing` on 2026-11-17 (decisions F-199 to F-202; D-076 in `DECISIONS.md`, proposed); not core, `src/obaaf/audit/` only. *What was built:* (1) a job-level `uses: $/...` is a call in this repository, as `./` is (the checker's reading, D-065): the called jobs get the caller's events and the caller the check names, its file is one GitHub may not run (whether a job's `uses:` takes `$/` was not established), and a `$/` caller never covers a called workflow's token for OBAAF-SET-012; `uses: ./` alone is a call to a file that was not read. (2) A job id with an ASCII character outside GitHub's form, or an ASCII digit or `-` first, makes the file refused (this also closes a forge of `HARMLESS_PROBLEM_RE` through the quoted id); a non-ASCII id, one of 100 characters or more, or an empty `uses` makes it one GitHub may not run (`may_refuse`). (3) Under `allowed_actions: selected` only a step's action of GitHub's own organisations (where allowed) or an entry equal to a plainly written reference counts as allowed; nothing while an entry holds `!`; another repository's reusable workflow never; under `selected` and `local_only` a local action, an image or an empty `uses` makes the file one GitHub may not run, and so does an unknown `allowed_actions` value. (4) A cycle of calls or a chain of more than three nested calls makes a file one GitHub may not run; spreading that to callers is linear (4,000 calls: 3.38 s before, 0.03 s now). (5) OBAAF-SET-007 weighs a job in a file GitHub may not run, and where the deciding route gives a finding, any other job that may report the check on a route that gives none, or a filtered route in a second file, makes it "cannot tell". *Review:* six passes by Opus 5.5 sub-agents that did not write it, each on the last fixes: 1, 5, 3, 3, 1 and 0 blocking findings. The first found that the slice's own new "may not run" files silenced OBAAF-SET-007, which is why (5) is in the slice (F-202); the fourth that taking `Docker://` for an image (as the checker does) made a file GitHub may refuse one that runs, so that change was taken out (#209). *Run:* 1952 tests locally; 41 new in `tests/test_settings_audit_gaps.py`, of which 32 fail on `5de5b8a` (7 are marked as holding before and after, and 2 guard against faults earlier commits of the slice made); CI green on `9b233fe` ([run 44005390612](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/44005390612)); `obaaf audit --from-snapshot --format json` on this repository and on Expectrail at `5d25639` byte-identical before and after (neither snapshot holds the Actions permissions, so item 3 is exercised only by the tests). *Not run:* GitHub's pages (each fetch timed out waiting for an approval, F-200); a live audit with a token; Windows. *Left:* #209 (seven older or over-cautious cases the reviews found), #125, #202.
- ☑ **2b.8 The settings report quotes the names it lists.** Merged as [#213](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/213) by run `pintail` on 2026-11-17 (merge `701a58f`), added under F-207 from #125 (found by 2b.6's review); decision D-078 in `DECISIONS.md` is proposed; plan decisions F-207 and F-208. *What changed:* in the "What was read" part of `obaaf audit`'s text report every name in a list (required checks, an environment's patterns, reviewers and other rules, secrets, a policy's events and paths) is printed with `quoted`; a single value (15 places, from a ruleset's enforcement to the header's address) is bare only when it matches `[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_.:/@+-]*`; `github.quoted` replaces more look-alikes of `"` and `\` with `?` (U+02F5, U+02F6, U+1CD3, U+2E42, the comma ornaments, eight backslash look-alikes, and any compatibility form holding either). `docs/expectrail-settings-audit.md` keeps its words of 2026-11-13 and says so; its test undoes the one change (quotes on names after the first on each line) and compares to the character (F-208). *Review:* three passes by one Opus 5.5 sub-agent that did not write it, with about 60 hostile snapshots and mutation tests: 3 blocking findings (a names-only record test that missed 15 of 20 record mutations; #125's example back in with U+02F6; most single values revertible with every test passing), then 2 (wording: the JSON does change for names holding a newly listed look-alike; U+030B and `''` still get through), then none. *Run:* 1981 tests locally; CI green on the head `8ffd7e7` ([run 44046686460](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/44046686460)), with the live audit ([44046686501](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/44046686501)) and drift runs. Before and after, the JSON is byte-identical on this repository and Expectrail (`3b42a95`); the text differs only in "What was read". *Left (in D-078):* the reasons a setting was not read, its needs, the accepted permissions, a problem's part, the reader's own `NOT READ: branch` label and `_job_label` are still printed with `plain`; right-to-left letters, wrapping, a combining mark after a space and two apostrophes.

**2c. One control that enforces**

- ☑ **2c.1 Design.** Merged as [#64](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/64): [egress-control.md](egress-control.md). An agent that did not write the note checked it against the pages in two passes: 10 blocking findings in the first (the note had described one of AWF's two modes as the mode, and its rule about later steps was unsound), 4 in the second, each corrected with the reviewer's own wording. CI passed on the head commit ([run 43152096573](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43152096573)). The action pins and wraps the Agent Workflow Firewall (decision F-20). **Nothing was run** (no Docker in the session), so "which mechanism, and what failed" is answered as: AWF, and nothing has failed because nothing has been tried. *What the reading settled:* AWF can be pinned and verified by its documentation (setup action at a commit, a `version` input, image digests in the `image-tag` output, cosign signatures, attested release assets), and its repository has no published advisory. *What it did not settle:* which mode the pinned version runs by default (the documentation site describes `sudo awf` with firewall rules; the notes for release v0.28.71 call that `--legacy-security` and say the default needs no `sudo`); whether `sudo` does anything inside the sandbox; what the command can write; and whether AWF holds against OBAAF's escape tests. *The open question that can sink a command wrapper:* the confinement ends when the command ends, and what the command wrote (the workspace, `/tmp`, parts of the home directory, perhaps a directory on the `PATH`) is still there for later steps and post steps, which have the full network. The note says when OBAAF would recommend a whole-job control instead. Read as asked: Cimon's repository page and `action.yml` (not its sensor's source), and a search for advisories against AWF (none; two dependency findings in its images). Not read: the source of Harden-Runner's agent. *Done when* a design note says which mechanism OBAAF uses on GitHub-hosted Linux runners, how the confined command is kept from undoing it (hosted runners have passwordless sudo), and where the other tools are the better choice. *Positioning (1.4), decision F-20:* the design starts from pinning and wrapping the Agent Workflow Firewall. A mechanism of OBAAF's own is designed only if AWF fails when it is tried against the escape list in slice 1.2's notes, or cannot be pinned and verified; the note says which, and what failed. Read Cimon's repository as well, and search for published advisories against AWF, which slice 1.2 did not do.
- ☑ **2c.2 The action and its escape tests.** Merged as [#119](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/119) by run `plover` (decisions F-75 to F-78). *What was built:* [`actions/egress`](../actions/egress/action.yml) runs one command under AWF v0.29.13 (binary by SHA-256, nine images by digest) in AWF's default network-isolation mode, with a fixed command line, plain host names only, no environment but named variables (names that change a shell, a loader or the network, names AWF reads as configuration, and model keys and GitHub tokens are refused), writes narrowed by AWF's `filesystem.allowWrite` to a scratch directory under `/tmp` (a copy of the workspace, the command's home and `TMPDIR`), AWF's own writable directories deleted after the run (with `sudo` where AWF made them as root) or the step fails, a probe inside the sandbox before the command, workflow commands stopped, and a failure if the runner's file-command files change; output `confined` tells the command's own status from the action's. [`.github/workflows/egress-escape-tests.yml`](../.github/workflows/egress-escape-tests.yml) tries 22 ways out, each first outside the sandbox (the control) and then inside, judged from a packet capture on the runner's external interface, plus the ways to leave something for a later step; a second job checks exit status, variables, refusals and cleanup; it runs on changes to the action and weekly. [egress-control.md](egress-control.md) is rewritten from the design note. Decision D-047 was accepted on 2026-11-13 (#130). *What was run:* escape tests on `ubuntu-24.04` (runs [43434321764](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43434321764) on the merged head `ce449f0`, [43432030855](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43432030855), [43431555704](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43431555704)): all 20 IPv4 attempts got out outside and were not seen leaving inside; the listed host answered 200; nothing a later step would run or load changed; `::set-env` and `::add-path` from inside had no effect with `ACTIONS_ALLOW_UNSECURE_COMMANDS` set. CI green on the merged head ([43434320499](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43434320499)); 1190 tests locally. *What running it settled* (2c.1's five questions): AWF v0.29.13 defaults to network isolation and needs no `sudo`; inside, `NoNewPrivs` is 1 and no capability is effective, so `sudo` refuses; AWF's defaults leave the workspace, the host's `/tmp` and the home's tool directories writable, enough to plant a program for a later step, which is why the action narrows writes; no IPv6 route inside; names the command resolves itself do not leave the runner. *Not testable:* IPv6 (the runner had no IPv6 route). *Recorded, not asserted, and it got out:* a host name asked of the proxy over HTTPS leaves the runner in a DNS query although the proxy refuses it (likely cause in AWF's Squid configuration: a `dst` rule for the API proxy); a different `Host` behind the listed name reached that server. *Review:* three passes by sub-agents that wrote none of it, all on Opus 4.8 (F-77): the first found one blocking fault (the command's home is backed by a directory under `RUNNER_TEMP` that was left behind) and six smaller; the second two blocking (the deletion would fail every run because AWF makes directories as root; AWF moves its log and session directories into the host's `/tmp`) and four smaller; the third, a fresh agent, one blocking (the page cited runs older than the checks it described) and three smaller; all fixed, and its check of the last commit found nothing. *Left for later:* 2c.4 (the DNS channel and model keys), 2c.3 (the checker), `ubuntu-22.04`, larger and self-hosted runners, an agent that is an action. *From 2c.1 (the specification it was built to):* build to the design note; run the pinned AWF first and settle its mode, `sudo`, where the command can write, IPv6 and DNS; answer whether the action can keep what the command wrote from later steps; the judge is a packet capture outside the sandbox, and every attempt is first made outside it. *Done when* an action in this repository runs a command with network access limited to a declared allowlist, blocks and logs everything else, and OBAAF's own CI has a job that tries to get out (a blocked hostname, a direct IP, another DNS resolver, IPv6, sudo, a child process) and fails on every attempt, plus a test that the allowed host still works; if full enforcement is not achievable on hosted runners, the documentation says exactly what is and is not blocked.
- ☑ **2c.3 The checker recognises it.** Merged as [#127](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/127) by run `whimbrel`, in Sean's live session, on 2026-11-13 (decisions F-87 to F-90; D-050 in `DECISIONS.md`, accepted on 2026-11-13 in #130). *What was built:* `obaaf check` reads every egress control an agent job might have and keeps the best (new module `src/obaaf/checker/egress.py`). GitHub's native egress firewall is verified when every run of the job is on `ubuntu-24.04-firewall` and `.github/egress-firewall.yaml` sets `mode: enforce`, with a token that has `contents`; the conditions come from its README at `240089d`. OBAAF's egress action is verified at a commit listed in `egress_action_releases` (empty), with a written allow-list that has no public suffix at or under a name, on hosted `ubuntu-24.04` and not in a container, with no step but `actions/upload-artifact` after or beside it, no earlier step that names `/tmp` or uses `&` or mentions a container engine, a multiplexer or a detaching command, and the agent as its command, read from where credentials go (a secret, `github.token`, `id-token: write` or `models` outside it is "not met"; undeclared permissions and a job with no credential at all, unless the agent is recognised inside, are "cannot tell"). Harden-Runner is "cannot tell" at best (the tier is not in the workflow) and "not met" at a listed release before v2.16.0, without `disable-sudo-and-containers: true`, or in a container on a hosted runner (`weak-monitor`); its version comes from 75 release commits in `detection.yaml`. AWF from a script is "cannot tell", or "not met" with `--enable-dind`. The public suffix list ships as `src/obaaf/data/public_suffix_list.dat` (MPL-2.0, F-88). The stale sentence is corrected in the five places. Guide: [checker-configuration.md](checker-configuration.md#egress-controls). *Run:* 1321 tests on the branch merged with `flagship` (Python 3.13, locally), 61 of them new; nine checks green on the head `1cd9856` ([run 43510801945](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43510801945)). The old and new checker gave the same findings, word for word, on this repository, its 16 risky fixtures and Expectrail's CI files at `0509d73`. *Review:* five passes by one Opus 5.5 sub-agent resumed with its context; the first four found six false passes, a 43-second regular expression on a 14 KB workflow, and steps that ran what the confined command wrote, all fixed and pinned by tests; the fifth found nothing blocking (F-89, F-90). *Not run:* GitHub's native firewall and Harden-Runner (their README and source were read). *Left:* listing a released commit of the action is a maintainer's act (item 25 of [Waiting on Sean](#waiting-on-sean)); `sudo -b`, a key in `vars.` or `needs.*.outputs`, and whether the confined command can reach a service container are named in the guide as not read; the release lists and the suffix list age. The original specification follows. *Done when* GHA-020 clears only when the agent step really runs under the control (structurally: the step, not a declaration), and a test shows that a look-alike does not clear it. *Positioning (1.4):* the checker recognises GitHub's native egress firewall (the runner label and a policy file in enforce mode), AWF (at a version OBAAF has tested, and not with `--enable-dind`, which its documentation says allows a bypass), and OBAAF's wrapper. It recognises Harden-Runner only when four things can be established: the version is 2.16.0 or later, `sudo` and containers are removed, the step comes before the agent, and the repository is one the tier in use covers (the free tier does not cover private repositories). The version is read from a list of release commits that OBAAF ships, never from a comment beside a pinned commit, which is text the workflow's author controls. Where any of this cannot be established the result is "cannot tell". The sentence that hosted runners have no native egress allow-list is corrected in the five places slice 1.2 lists. *From 2c.1:* for OBAAF's own action the rule clears only when the action is pinned to a commit OBAAF lists as released; the agent command is inside the action's `run` input with no agent in another step of the job; the allow-list is written out in the workflow, with no single-label name and no name on the public suffix list, private section included (a name allows all its subdomains), and the report shows it; and nothing after the confined step, post steps included, runs or loads anything from a place the command could write, or saves such a place to a cache. That last condition cannot be read until 2c.2 shows which places those are; until then the result is "cannot tell". The checker recognises few agents by name, so "no agent in another step" is read as: no other step of the job is given a secret and job-level `env` holds none. That rests on an agent needing a key; decide here what to say of a job whose agent needs none. The action wraps a command, so it cannot cover an agent that is itself an action; those jobs clear only by a control that covers the whole job. *From 2c.2:* the places the command can write that outlive the step are now known: the action's `workspace` and `scratch` outputs, under one directory `/tmp/obaaf-egress.*`; AWF's own writable directories are deleted by the action, which fails the step if it cannot. So the last condition can be read: no later step, post steps included, runs or loads anything from those outputs or that directory (running `git` in the copy counts: the command can write its hooks and configuration), or caches them. The report beside a cleared GHA-020 must say that a name asked of the proxy leaves the runner in a DNS query until 2c.4 closes it. The action refuses model-key names, so a job whose agent needs a key cannot use it yet; say "cannot tell" or "not met" for such a job, not "verified". The released commits are the ones the escape tests passed on; this repository has not listed any yet.
- ☑ **2c.4 The proxy's DNS channel, and model keys through AWF's API proxy.** Merged as [#128](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/128) by run `dotterel` on 2026-11-13 (decisions F-92 to F-94; D-051 in `DECISIONS.md`, proposed). *What was built:* `actions/egress` starts a resolver of its own for each run (`egress.py dns-filter`, standard library only) on Docker's `docker0` address, port 53, bound with `sudo` and then run as `nobody`, and gives it to AWF with `--dns-servers`; it forwards a query, rebuilt from its ID, recursion flag and one question, only when the name is covered by the allow-list or is exactly the API host of a model key given, with plain labels only, and answers `REFUSED` otherwise; the action checks before the command that it refuses the probe's name and answers the first listed one, and fails the step (status 3) if not or if it stops early; outputs `dns-refused` and `dns-log`. New input `model-keys` (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`) puts the keys in AWF's environment for its API proxy; the command gets base URLs and placeholders; AWF's hosted-web policy is `enabled: false`. Escape tests: four new asserted DNS attempts (a labelled name asked of the proxy over HTTPS and over plain HTTP, required among the resolver's refusals; two lookups by the command, which go to Docker's resolver and are regression tests), the judge fails on any unlisted name in a DNS query, 2c.2's plain-HTTP attempt now names the proxy (curl reads only the lower-case `http_proxy`, which AWF does not set, so it had gone direct), the listed host may answer with any code (it answered 403, rate-limited, inside and outside the sandbox once), and a behaviour step sends requests with made-up keys. 2c.3's checker messages, guide and the landscape row were reworded where this slice made them untrue; the checker's logic is unchanged (F-92). *Run:* escape tests green on the merged head `cb3dfc8` ([43516620266](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43516620266)) and on `27f339a`, `d1c7b14`, `873c375`: the 24 IPv4 attempts got out outside and were not seen leaving inside, the two IPv6 ones were not testable, no unlisted name left the runner in a DNS query, and the resolver refused the two labelled names; Anthropic answered 401 `authentication_error` and OpenAI 401 `invalid_api_key` through the sidecar with neither in `allow`; a `web_fetch` tool got 403 `claude_hosted_web_disabled` from the sidecar; the proxy's log shows the sidecar's `CONNECT … 200` to both hosts; neither key was in the command's environment, in the 1278 readable files searched, or in the logs. CI green on every head ([43516620298](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43516620298) on the last). 1336 tests locally with `PYTHONPATH=src`. *Review:* three passes by one Opus 5.5 sub-agent resumed with its context: the first found four blocking faults (the plain-HTTP attempt never reached the proxy; the docs said the command's own lookups went to the new resolver; stale changelog lines; a label ending in a newline passed) and five smaller (raw queries forwarded, provider subdomains answered, hosted-web wording, weak checks, wording); the second found all fixed and blocked on a missing run and on 2c.3's text; the third on D-050's wording and the missing 2c.5; all fixed, the last commit wording only. *Not run:* a real key or a model call that succeeds; OpenAI's hosted-web refusal; IPv6; `ubuntu-22.04`, larger and self-hosted runners. *Left, and stated in [egress-control.md](egress-control.md):* data in a name under a listed name reaches that domain's DNS servers; with a key, anything sent to the provider, remote MCP servers and URLs a provider is asked to fetch, bodies AWF's proxy cannot parse as JSON and WebSocket upgrades are not refused; the checker does not read `model-keys` (2c.5). Whether to tell AWF's maintainers about the resolve-before-deny order is for Sean (F-94). The original specification follows. *Done when* either the escape test that asks the proxy for a labelled name asserts that the label does not leave the runner, or the documentation says exactly why it cannot be closed on hosted runners and what the channel carries; and either a model key reaches AWF's API proxy and an agent's request through it reaches the provider (a dummy key that gets the provider's own refusal is enough), with the documentation saying what the agent sees and that the sidecar can reach that provider whatever `allow` says, or the documentation says why the action does not support it. *From 2c.2:* the leak shows in run 43425789742 ("the proxy: a name the command asked it for, and was refused, left the runner in a DNS query: True"); AWF v0.29.13's Squid configuration has `acl allow_api_proxy_ip dst <address>` before the rules that refuse unlisted names (`src/squid/config-sections.ts`), so Squid resolves every name first, which is the likely cause and was not tested on its own; whether a configuration file passed with `--config` can change that order, or whether a resolver that answers only listed names can be given to Squid, was not looked at. AWF reads model keys for its sidecar only from its own process environment (`src/commands/resolve-credentials.ts`), and the action starts AWF with `PATH`, `HOME`, `TMPDIR` and `LANG` only. Do not report the leak upstream without Sean: decide in this slice whether to ask him to.
- ☑ **2c.5 The checker weighs `model-keys`.** Merged as [#134](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/134) by run `greenshank` on 2026-11-14 (decisions F-98 to F-102; D-052 in `DECISIONS.md`, proposed). *What was built:* `obaaf check` places each credential given to a step that uses the egress action, reading it as the action reads its inputs (`_placement` in `src/obaaf/checker/rules.py`): a credential in the step's `env` under a name `model-keys` names goes to AWF's API proxy, and the provider's API host is shown after the allow-list as `api.anthropic.com (through AWF's API proxy)`, one of the finding's facts; one under a name the `env` input names, written in `run`, or a step-`env` credential written into `run` through `${{ env.KEY }}`, is passed to the command; one under neither name and in no input reaches neither and is no evidence. **A key given to the proxy and a credential passed to the command both leave the control verifiable** (the second goes beyond this entry's wording: F-98). Names the action refuses in ways the checker reads are "not met"; computed inputs, a computed step `env` holding a credential, names differing only in case or not written as text, the whole env context in `run`, and a credential or env value in another input or a condition are "cannot tell". Beside a cleared finding: the credentials passed to the command (a listed host can be made to act with them), the provider channel for each model key (remote MCP servers, URLs; only the hosted web tools are refused), and the job's log and artifacts; the native firewall's text names the log and artifacts too. OBAAF-GHA-005's fourth control says, whatever its verdict, where each placed credential goes, what could not be placed and what would make the action refuse; no control is added to GHA-005 (F-99, slice 2c.7). `egress_action_floor: f0bfbd7…` in `detection.yaml`, with a test that refuses a listed commit that is not a descendant of it, whose copied lists differ from the checker's, or whose input parsing or action.yml inputs differ from the floor's, failing closed without history; CI's test job fetches the full history (`fetch-depth: 0`) and the test ran there. The checker copies `MODEL_KEYS`, `WITHHELD_BY_AWF` and the name pattern from the action, kept equal by a test. Guides: [checker-configuration.md](checker-configuration.md#egress-controls), [egress-control.md](egress-control.md#what-the-checker-credits-slice-2c3). *Run:* 1354 tests locally (Python 3.13; 18 new, one rewritten); nine checks green on the head `b6700c8`, Python 3.10 and 3.13 ([run 43538427809](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43538427809)). Same findings, facts, messages, states and control verdicts before and after on this repository, its 16 risky fixtures and a copy of Expectrail at `c8a9042` (none uses the action). On a scratch adaptation of Expectrail's `byo-live.yml` under the action with `model-keys` and a made-up listed commit: "not met" as first adapted (a key check in another step, and `npm install -g @anthropic-ai/claude-code`), GHA-020 fixed once both were removed. *Review:* three passes by one Opus 5.5 sub-agent resumed with its context (F-102): a key written into `run` through `${{ env.KEY }}` placed with the proxy (a regression), then a `true:` key compared as `"True"`; both fixed and tested; the third pass found nothing blocking. *Not run:* a real workflow with `model-keys` on GitHub; a listed commit (none is listed). *Left:* 2c.7 and 2c.8 (new, core); issue #136 (does a credential `actions/checkout` persists outside `.git/config` reach the confined command; F-101); a credential in a computed `allow` is said in GHA-020's control but not among GHA-005's "could not place"; the floor test does not compare `run()` or action.yml's wiring, which the escape tests a listed commit must pass do. The original specification follows. Found by 2c.4 (decision F-92). Slice 2c.4 added the action's input `model-keys`, which gives `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` or `OPENAI_API_KEY` to AWF's API proxy and never to the command, and closed the proxy's DNS channel for unlisted names. 2c.3's checker reads neither: any credential in the confined step's `env` makes `egress-enforced` "cannot tell", and its text was only reworded in 2c.4. *Done when* the checker reads `model-keys`; tells a key given there from one passed to the command in `env`; decides, says in a Proposed decision, and tests whether a step whose only credentials are in `model-keys` can have `egress-enforced` verified, given that the API proxy reaches the provider's API host whatever `allow` says and that a provider can be asked to connect elsewhere (remote MCP servers and URLs to fetch are not refused; [egress-control.md](egress-control.md)); says in GHA-005's controls where such a key goes; names the provider channel beside a cleared finding where a key is given; and makes the list of released commits of the action (`egress_action_releases`) take only commits at or after 2c.4's merge, because the text beside a cleared finding is true only for those. *Needs:* 2c.3 and 2c.4. *Core:* yes (`src/obaaf/checker/`).
- ☑ **2c.6 The egress action reviewed on Opus 5.5.** Merged as [#135](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/135) by run `whimbrel` in Sean's session on 2026-11-14 (decisions F-107, F-108; D-054 in `DECISIONS.md`, proposed). Sean chose to have the egress action reviewed again on Opus 5.5, where 2c.2's three passes were on Opus 4.8 (plan item 22). *What was built:* the review found no way past the network rules and two ways out through the runner's disk, the first confirmed on a hosted runner. (1) The runner's own .NET processes listen on diagnostic sockets in `/tmp`, which AWF mounts into the sandbox; from outside the sandbox two answered a `ProcessInfo` request (Runner.Listener and Runner.Worker) and four debugger pipes were writable. A read-only mount does not stop a connection to a socket, and that protocol can make a .NET program dump its memory or load a library. The action now sets every such socket and pipe unwritable to the command for the run and the probe fails if the command can still write one; inside, none could connect. (2) A link the command leaves in the copy is deleted after the run (new output `removed`), so a later step that reads or uploads the copy cannot be pointed at the runner's files. Also: the kept-token refusal now reads checkout v6's `includeIf` and `config.worktree`; `denied` takes the request's target, not the `Host` header; the checker's release floor moves to `7c1bded`, the slice's last change to the action, so no commit with these holes can be listed. *Run:* 1372 tests locally; the escape tests green on hosted `ubuntu-24.04` ([run 43547361328](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43547361328)), 29 attempts, the three new ones blocked inside. *Review:* four passes by one Opus 5.5 sub-agent (model "opus"); the first found the two escapes, the second a blocking fail-open in the unlock, both fixed; the last two nothing blocking. *Not run:* a memory dump or a library load from inside the sandbox; larger or self-hosted runners. *Left:* the AWF finding is with GitHub (item 29 of [Waiting on Sean](#waiting-on-sean)); D-054 for a maintainer; the checker's text beside a cleared finding does not yet mention a socket a program creates in `/tmp` after the probe. *From 2c.5:* 2c.8 took decision number D-053 while this was in review, so this slice's decision is D-054.
- ☑ **2c.7 OBAAF-GHA-005 and a key the agent cannot read.** Merged as [#144](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/144) by run `sandpiper` on 2026-11-14 (decisions F-113 to F-116; D-055 in `DECISIONS.md`, proposed). *What was built:* a fifth control under OBAAF-GHA-005, `key-out-of-reach`: the agent's command cannot read any of the job's secrets (`GITHUB_TOKEN` aside, OBAAF-GHA-003's). It is verified only where every secret is set in the `env` of steps that use OBAAF's egress action at a listed commit, on `ubuntu-24.04`, and goes to AWF's API proxy (`model-keys`) or to nothing the command runs, and nothing found before or beside the step that holds the key could reach it there. *Not met:* a secret passed to the command (`env`, `run`, `${{ env.KEY }}`) or given anywhere else in the job; no step that uses the action (GitHub's native firewall holds no key, so OBAAF-GHA-020 can clear there and OBAAF-GHA-005 cannot); a recognised agent or a credential in a step that can run before or beside the key-holding step; `id-token: write` or the `models` permission. *Cannot tell:* what cannot be placed; an unlisted ref; another runner or a container; a variable bash or the loader obeys (`BASH_ENV`, `PATH`, `LD_PRELOAD` and others) or `ACTIONS_ALLOW_UNSECURE_COMMANDS` in an `env` (#139); a step before or beside it whose script names `GITHUB_ENV` or `GITHUB_PATH` or may leave a process running, or that runs beside it; service containers with volumes or options; undeclared token permissions; a key that reaches nothing with no agent recognised. Where each credential given to the action goes moved from the fourth control's detail to the fifth's. *The three accounts:* the step still holding the key is said beside a verified verdict and what runs before or beside it is read; a key written into `run` is "not met" while the network control can still be verified (D-052); the native firewall is "not met". *Baselines:* none stops matching (controls are not part of a finding's identity), and a job that moves its key from the command to the proxy keeps its acceptance; tested. The finding still cannot be cleared: `environment-restricted` is never verified. *How it was checked:* 1392 tests locally on Python 3.13 (1372 before; 20 new); nine checks green on the merged head `49a4ba8` ([run 43555504192](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43555504192)); the old and new checker on 25 targets (this repository, its 16 risky fixtures, its 6 templates, Expectrail at `13e8f70` with and without its baseline) gave the same findings, messages, states and exit statuses, the only difference being `key-out-of-reach: not met` on each OBAAF-GHA-005 that lists controls. *The review:* three passes by one Opus 5.5 sub-agent that wrote none of it, about 80 probe workflows. The first found two blocking classes, both false "verified": an agent outside the sandbox before or beside the action (an agent CLI or action in the step before, a parallel sibling, an agent given `github.token`), and steps the control did not read (a decoy use of the action first, an unpinned use before, parallel siblings, a privileged service container). The second found two more: an install step 2c.8 accepts switched off the rule for a key that reaches nothing, and `ACTIONS_ALLOW_UNSECURE_COMMANDS` was not read. All fixed and tested; the third pass found nothing blocking, and its one note became a code comment. Issue #145 (the action's `uname` found through the `PATH`) came from the first pass. *Not run:* a real workflow with `model-keys` (no commit is listed). *Not read, and said in the docs:* what an action used before the step leaves running or writes to `GITHUB_ENV`, `GITHUB_PATH` or the home directory; writes or detached processes spelt without the words the checker looks for; an accepted install's own scripts; a later action's `pre:` step. *The specification was:* Found by 2c.5 (decision F-99). OBAAF-GHA-005 says an agent job receives a secret, and is cleared only when its four controls are verified; none of them asks whether the agent can read the key. Since 2c.5 the fourth control's detail says that a key named in the egress action's `model-keys` goes to AWF's API proxy, out of the command's reach, but nothing in the finding's state changes for it. The other way round, a key the agent can read can leave through the job's log or an artifact whatever the network allows, so `egress-enforced` protects such a key less than its place among GHA-005's controls suggests (2c.5 says so beside a cleared GHA-020; GHA-005 does not). *Done when* a Proposed decision says, and tests show, how OBAAF-GHA-005 treats a secret the agent's command cannot read (a fifth control, a case of its own, or a changed set of controls) and a secret it can; it accounts for the step still holding the key, for a workflow that writes the key into `run` (2c.5 reports that as passed to the command), and for GitHub's native firewall, where no proxy holds the key; and it keeps existing baseline entries matching or says which stop. *Needs:* 2c.5. *Core:* yes.
- ☑ **2c.8 Installing an agent's CLI is not running it.** Merged as [#138](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/138) by run `greenshank` on 2026-11-14, taken under F-31 ahead of 2c.7 (decisions F-103 to F-106; D-053 in `DECISIONS.md`, proposed). *What was built:* `_installs_only` in `src/obaaf/checker/rules.py` reads a step before the egress action and vouches for it when every command in its shell script is a global install of a package in the new `agent_installs` (`@anthropic-ai/claude-code`, `@openai/codex`, `@google/gemini-cli`; `npm install -g`, `pnpm add -g`, `yarn global add`, `bun add -g`; an `@version` and options that take no value allowed; `sudo` only with `-E`, `-H` or `-n`) or the CLI's `--version`; the step must be a plain `bash` or `sh` step without `env` or `working-directory`, the job and workflow must set no `env` and no `defaults.run`, and the script may hold no `$`, quote, pipe, redirection, `&`, glob, backslash or trailing comment. Such a step is no longer an agent outside the confined command. A step that names a listed package in any other way is "cannot tell" (`_package_mention_only`), unless it also runs an agent (another pattern, `npx`, `npm exec`, `pnpm dlx`, `yarn dlx`, `bunx`, or a path into the package), which stays "not met", as does any such step after the action. Which jobs are agent jobs does not change. Guide: [checker-configuration.md](checker-configuration.md#egress-controls). *Run:* 1361 tests locally (Python 3.13); nine checks green on the head `fe74150`, Python 3.10 and 3.13 ([run 43542142215](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43542142215)). Same findings before and after on this repository, its 16 risky fixtures and a copy of Expectrail at `c8a9042`. The scratch adaptation of Expectrail's `byo-live.yml` with its install step as written (`sudo npm install -g @anthropic-ai/claude-code`, `claude --version`) clears GHA-020 once an earlier step's `bwrap --tmpfs /tmp` is taken out. *Review:* three passes by the same Opus 5.5 sub-agent as 2c.5, resumed (F-106): the first found accepted install steps that could run the author's code (the step's `env`, a default shell, options that take a path whose value can be a listed package's name, installs with options alone or without `-g`, `sudo -b`, `-i`, `-s`); the second found the job's and workflow's `env`; the third nothing blocking. The commit after it was wording only. *Not read, and stated:* what earlier steps write to `GITHUB_ENV`, `GITHUB_PATH` or the home directory, and package-manager configuration in the working directory (`pnpm`, `yarn`, `bun`). *Left:* issue #139 (the action's own bash steps source `BASH_ENV` from the job's environment, outside the sandbox; for the action). The original specification follows. Found by 2c.5 (decision F-100). The egress action's control is "not met" when another step runs an agent the checker recognises, and `agent_commands` in `detection.yaml` has `@anthropic-ai/claude-code` (and `@openai/codex`, `@google/gemini-cli`), which matches `npm install -g @anthropic-ai/claude-code`. So the natural way to use the action with Claude Code, installing it in one step and running it confined in the next, cannot be cleared: on a scratch adaptation of the first adopter's `byo-live.yml` it was the one condition left once the rest was fixed. *Done when* a step before the confined one that only installs an agent's package (and is given no credential, which 2c.3 already requires) is told from one that runs the agent, by reading the command rather than by a pattern that `npm install -g … && claude -p …` could ride along with, and is "cannot tell" where it cannot be told; tests show that `npx @anthropic-ai/claude-code -p …`, an install followed by a run in the same step, and an install after the confined step still count; and which jobs are agent jobs does not change. *Needs:* 2c.5. *Core:* yes.
- ☑ **2c.9 The egress action's own steps.** Merged as [#190](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/190) by run `merganser` on 2026-11-16, added under F-172 from #139, #145 and #136 (decisions F-172 to F-176; D-069 in `DECISIONS.md`, proposed). *What was built:* the action's three steps run outside the sandbox holding the step's `env`, model keys included. Each now has `/usr/bin/python3 -I -S {0}` as its shell and only runs `egress.py` through `runpy`, so no bash, no `PATH` lookup and no `PYTHON*` variable or `site` directory has any effect; each step's `env` empties 32 of the 34 variables glibc 2.39's `ld.so` strips for setuid programs (`LOADER_VARIABLES`; `LD_SHOW_AUXV` and `LD_DYNAMIC_WEAK` are left alone because the loader obeys them when merely present, and they load no file); `egress.py` refuses with `refused=environment` if one still has a value; the machine is read from `os.uname()`. A fourth step, `if: ${{ !cancelled() && steps.run.outcome == 'success' && steps.run.outputs.confined != 'true' }}`, has a shell that does not exist, so the runner fails it without starting a program: the answer to `LD_TRACE_LOADED_OBJECTS`, which no `env` can unset and which ends every program the loader starts with status 0. The escape tests' new job `own-steps` plants `BASH_ENV`, `SHELLOPTS`+`PS4`, `LD_PRELOAD`, `LD_AUDIT`, `PYTHONPATH` and programs first on the `PATH` (job `env`, `GITHUB_ENV`, `GITHUB_PATH`, the step's `env`) and requires all 8 to fire for a plain step, the 6 bash, loader and `PATH` hooks to fire for the action at `7c1bded`, none for the action now, the floor to succeed without running and the action now to fail under `LD_TRACE_LOADED_OBJECTS`, and the runner's loader to strip no name the action neither empties nor names. The behaviour job answers #136: the credential file a default checkout keeps in `RUNNER_TEMP`, and marked files there, in `~`, `~/.config` and beside the workspace, were absent inside the sandbox (1,450 readable files searched for the mark). *Run:* 1824 tests locally (Python 3.13); all 12 checks green on the last head `c1b590c` (CI [43784805638](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43784805638), escape tests [43784805659](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43784805659)); the facts above are from [43780561661](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43780561661) and [43782953569](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43782953569). *Review:* three passes by an Opus 5.5 sub-agent, none blocking; its notes (an empty `LD_SHOW_AUXV` printed the auxiliary vector, the guard's `success()`, a floor on the files searched, wording) were fixed. *Not done:* the checker still reads every commit of the action as it reads `7c1bded` (2c.10); an earlier step with `sudo`, or a process it leaves running, is not covered by the action (the checker reads earlier steps for those). Guide: [egress-control.md](egress-control.md), item 12.
- ☑ **2c.10a The checker reads the action from 2c.9 on.** Merged as [#191](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/191) by run `merganser` on 2026-11-16, split from 2c.10 (F-177). *What was built:* `egress_action_floor` in `src/obaaf/data/detection.yaml` moves from `7c1bded` to `c1b590c`, 2c.9's last change to the action; the floor test names it, refuses `278670d` (the 2c.9 commit just before it) and `7c1bded`; the rule's text in `rules.yaml` (and `spec/checker-rules.md`), the two guides, the changelog, D-069 and two comments say so, and the escape tests' "before" steps are named for `7c1bded`. No commit is listed and no finding changes (this repository, and Expectrail at `a98dcb0` with its own CI command, byte-identical before and after). *Run:* 1824 tests locally; all 12 checks green on `86646ff` (CI [43786528816](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43786528816), escape tests [43786528800](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43786528800)). *Review:* two passes by an Opus 5.5 sub-agent (2 blocking findings, both stale floor wording, fixed; then none).
- ☑ **2c.10b What still makes `key-out-of-reach` "cannot tell"** (core). Merged as [#193](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/193) by run `goldeneye` on 2026-11-16 (decision D-070, proposed; issue #192). *What was decided:* now that every listable commit of the action runs its own steps as `/usr/bin/python3 -I -S`, starts every program with its own `env` and a fixed `PATH`, and empties the loader's variables (2c.9), the reasons dropped from `key-out-of-reach` are bash's, Python's, the `PATH`'s and the loader's variables in an `env` (`_LOADED_BY_BASH`, now gone) and an earlier step that writes `GITHUB_PATH`, calls `addPath` or names `BASH_ENV`. Kept: `GITHUB_ENV`/`exportVariable`/`_runner_file_commands` (read in a step's script, `env` values and inputs), `ACTIONS_ALLOW_UNSECURE_COMMANDS`, a computed `env`, a process left running. Added: any `env` key beginning `OPENSSL_` (case/space/expression) but the six build-tool names libcrypto and libssl do not read (`OPENSSL_DIR`, `_LIB_DIR`, `_INCLUDE_DIR`, `_STATIC`, `_NO_VENDOR`, `_ROOT_DIR`, `_LIBS`), because `egress.py` imports `hashlib` and `OPENSSL_CONF` (or Ubuntu's `OPENSSL_FORCE_FIPS_MODE`/`OPENSSL_FIPS_MODE_SWITCH_PATH` with `OPENSSL_MODULES`) makes libcrypto load a module the job names into the key-holding Python — reproduced outside GitHub on Ubuntu 24.04, Python 3.12, libssl3 3.0.13, the action's own first step included; not shown on a GitHub-hosted runner. The action's own fix is the new slice **2c.11** (not core); after it the floor moves and the `OPENSSL_` names can be dropped. A listing guard (`own_steps_differ`) pins a listable commit's action to the floor: the steps' shell, loader set, `run` text and last step; every program started a way the floor uses, with a clean dict `env=` or a named dict whose whole enclosing function is AST-identical to the floor's (the command launch `env=child_env` in `run()`); no `getattr` on subprocess/os/posix; `which` with `path=`; and the `FIXED_PATH` constant, `parse_env_names` and `parse_model_keys` identical to the floor's. *Run:* 1834 tests on Python 3.13 (1824 before); `obaaf docs build --check`, `obaaf catalog`, `obaaf check . --fail-on medium` exit 0; this repository and Expectrail at `9773ae5c` (a copy without `fresh-*`) byte-identical before and after; CI green on #193's head `715820b` ([run 43805401969](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43805401969)). *Review:* one Opus 5.5 sub-agent that did not write the change attacked it over seven passes; it broke the guard many ways (module alias, `posix`, `getattr`, `env=dict(os.environ)`, `{**os.environ}`, `.update(os.environ)`, a poisoned `model_keys`, a `FIXED_PATH` shadow) and reproduced the OpenSSL finding; every bypass is fixed and a mutant test, and its last pass found nothing blocking. *Not done:* the action still hands OpenSSL's variables to libcrypto (2c.11); #194 (AWF's teardown `HOME`) is recorded, not settled. Guide: [egress-control.md](egress-control.md) item 12, [checker-configuration.md](checker-configuration.md).
- ☑ **2c.11a The egress action removes OpenSSL's variables before libcrypto is initialised** (not core). Merged as [#196](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/196) by run `shelduck` on 2026-11-16, split from 2c.11 (F-178; decision D-071, proposed; issue #195). *What was built:* `actions/egress/egress.py` imports `os` first and, before importing `hashlib`, `secrets` or `urllib.request` (the imports that initialise the runner's libcrypto in the key-holding step), deletes from its own process every variable whose name, trimmed and upper-cased, begins `OPENSSL_` — a superset of the exact names libcrypto reads. So a workflow's `OPENSSL_CONF` (a config that loads a provider or engine module) or FIPS pair (`OPENSSL_MODULES` with `OPENSSL_FORCE_FIPS_MODE`/`OPENSSL_FIPS_MODE_SWITCH_PATH`, which loads `fips.so`) no longer loads a module into that process. The removal touches only the action's own step process, not the job's other steps or the command AWF runs (a clean env of its own); `SSL_CERT_FILE`/`SSL_CERT_DIR`, also read by libcrypto, are left (they redirect trust, not code, and the one download, AWF's binary, is SHA-256 pinned). The dynamic loader's variables stay emptied in `action.yml` (ld.so reads them pre-Python). A new `openssl-modules` escape job plants a marker module (it writes one line when libcrypto loads it, only in a process that holds `OBAAF_EGRESS_SCRIPT`) and sets `OPENSSL_CONF` and the FIPS pair through the job's `env`, `GITHUB_ENV` and the action step's `env`. *Run:* on GitHub-hosted `ubuntu-24.04` ([run 43817685428](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43817685428), job [133133371353](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43817685428/job/133133371353)) the module loaded for a plain step and for the action at the floor `c1b590c`, and for none of the action's steps now, while the action still ran its command in a sandbox that held (`confined=true`, exit 0). 1838 tests on Python 3.13 (1834 before; the four new ones fail on the floor's `egress.py`); `obaaf docs build --check`, `obaaf catalog`, `obaaf check . --fail-on medium` exit 0; actionlint clean; reproduced locally on Ubuntu 24.04, Python 3.12, libssl3 3.0.13. All 13 checks green on #196. *Review:* one Opus 5.5 sub-agent that did not write it (0 blocking; five non-blocking nits applied — the `SSL_CERT_*` note, a firmed-up unit test, and dropping a stale build-tool count from the re-touched doc sentence). *Not done (the rest of 2c.11, now core row 2c.11b):* `egress_action_floor` still points at `c1b590c`, which does not have this fix, so no commit of the action is listed and the checker still gives "cannot tell" for `OPENSSL_` names (D-070); moving the floor to a 2c.11a commit and dropping the `OPENSSL_` names from `key-out-of-reach` is 2c.11b, which the escape run above now unblocks. #194 (AWF teardown `HOME`) is still recorded, not settled. Guide: [egress-control.md](egress-control.md) item 12.
- ☑ **2c.11b The checker's floor is 2c.11a's change, and `OPENSSL_` names no longer count** (core). Merged as [#199](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/199) by run `pochard` on 2026-11-16 (decision D-072, proposed; issue #195, closed; F-179 to F-182). *What was built:* `egress_action_floor` moves from `c1b590c` to `cc2ba59`, the one commit of 2c.11a that touches `actions/egress`. `key-out-of-reach` drops `_REACHES_OWN_STEPS_PREFIXES` and the seven build-tool exceptions (D-070 said "six"; corrected there; item 48 below still says six). Still "cannot tell": `ACTIONS_ALLOW_UNSECURE_COMMANDS`, a key or an `env` computed when the job runs, a `GITHUB_ENV`/`exportVariable`/`_runner_file_commands` write before or beside the step (its detail now says the checker does not read which variables the write sets, and one may be `ACTIONS_ALLOW_UNSECURE_COMMANDS`), and a process left running. In the `env` of an earlier step, `ACTIONS_ALLOW_UNSECURE_COMMANDS` is read with space around it or as an expression key (letter case already was), and an `env` that is not a mapping is "cannot tell" (it was read as no `env`, which could give "verified"). An `OPENSSL_` name under the action's `env` input is "not met" at a listed commit, as the action refuses it (`removed_before_inputs`; #197 is about the action's message). The listing test (`own_steps_differ`) now ends with `action_differs`: a listed commit's `egress.py` (syntax tree, docstrings aside, parsed from its bytes; UTF-8 with no coding declaration) and `action.yml` (as data, descriptions aside) must be the floor's.
