CI was red on two intermediate heads: `12c343e` (the guide's table read as entries) and `12284ed` (`test (3.10)`: the 30 s and 8 s cost tests took 34.2 s and 9.0 s, from a gate that did work before its cheap check; fixed in `2cd7be6`). *Not run:* ci-agent-audit (no PyPI or npm); the real CLIs (reviewer A used stub CLIs, commander 13 and yargs 18); GitHub itself on these forms, including whether a step's `env` wins over `GITHUB_ENV`. *Left:* 2d.5c2b, 2d.5c2c; #254 (a quoted variable an agent CLI's parser may read as an option); stated limits: a program name built at run time, `--settings`, Codex `--sandbox danger-full-access` and `-c`, CLI environment variables, `python -m`, repository scripts, `BASH_ENV`, local Docker and JavaScript actions. Also fixed: GHA-025's env reader raised on an integer too long for Python to print.
- ☑ **2d.5c2b The other 20 agent tools' settings and options** (core; #81, open for 2d.5c2c). Merged as [#263](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/263) by run `chiffchaff` on 2026-11-19 (merge `8263c40`; decisions F-279 to F-281; D-094 in `DECISIONS.md`, proposed; issues #264 and #265 opened). *Done when* (as written): the other 20 entries' actions have their documented settings and their CLIs' documented options filled the same way, each citing its sentence from the tool's own files at a commit, or the entry says the tool documents none; a sub-agent that did not write it has tried to get past it. *What was built:* the twenty entries' action files, READMEs, documentation and CLI help text were read on 2026-11-19 at the commits in their `sources` (plus `github/docs` `39c1e01` for Copilot CLI, `OpenHands/docs` `e19cd0c`, `OpenHands/OpenHands-CLI` `283c6ac`, `kirodotdev/Kiro` `efac9e3`, `astral-sh/setup-uv` v6 `d0cc045`). Settings (medium): `gemini_debug: true` (run-gemini-cli) and `qwen_debug: true` (qwen-code-action), read as the runner's `fromJSON` reads them (`json: true`: first token, either quotes or up to a separator; a leading comment is "cannot tell"), and `enable-uv-cache: true` or `auto` (OpenHands pr-review). Options, high: Qwen Code's `--yolo`/`-y`/`--y`/`--approval-mode yolo`; Kilo Code's `--auto` and hidden `--yolo`, `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, `--dangerouslySkipPermissions`; Cline's `--yolo`, `-y`, `--zen`, `-z`, `--auto-approve`/`--autoapprove` with any value but `false` (new flag field `unless`); Copilot CLI's `--allow-all-tools`, `--allow-all`, `--yolo`; OpenHands' `--always-approve`, `--yolo`, `--headless` and any beginning of them (new flag field `prefixes`, argparse); PR-Agent's `--config-branch=$GITHUB_HEAD_REF` (an unreadable value such as `${{ github.head_ref }}` is medium "cannot tell"). Options, medium: Qwen's `--insecure`; Copilot's `--show-secrets`, `--trust-folder`, `--share-gist`, `--share`; `cn --auto`; OpenCode's `--hostname 0.0.0.0`; goose's `--dangerously-unauthenticated`. New entry fields: `no_warnings` (eleven entries say what was read and why nothing is reported; `obaaf catalog` refuses an entry with none of settings, flags and `no_warnings`, or with `no_warnings` beside them) and `shared_program`/`other_program` (AWS Copilot and pressly goose share `copilot` and `goose`: "cannot tell" is dropped only where every place the step names the program is followed by the other tool's own words). Reader fixes for every CLI: a one-letter group with `=` (`-dy=true`); a versioned or extras program after `uvx`, `uv … run` (uv's options anywhere) or `pipx run`. Three older entries list the files they cite among `sources`; OBAAF-GHA-026's `Why:` changed. *Review:* four passes by one Opus 5.5 sub-agent that wrote none of it, testing against the tools' parsers in the clones (6 groups, 7, 2 and 0 blocking findings); every fix is a test in `tests/test_agent_tool_settings_rest.py`. *Run:* every new quote and line number checked against the clones; 2,491 tests OK locally on `357a6dc`, `c80f1bc` and `f521bd1` (on `932fa64` two `test_docs` tests failed because `rules.yaml` was edited during the run; they pass on the head); `obaaf catalog`, `obaaf docs build --check`, `site/build.py` exit 0; this repository's and Expectrail's (at `8c2f9a20`, read-only copy without `fresh-*`, its CI command) text, JSON and `--lanes` byte-identical to `flagship` `4b35423`; CI 11 of 11 green on all five heads ([run 44503684675](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/44503684675) on `f521bd1`). *Not run:* the real CLIs; GitHub itself; ci-agent-audit (no PyPI or npm). *Left:* 2d.5c2c; #264 (a program name as another command's argument is read as the program, D-090's design, now with more names); #265 (a warned mode a tool runs in by default: a one-shot `cline`, and the three actions that run `--yolo` themselves); Kiro CLI and Amp (documented only on web pages); CodeRabbit's reviewer (its repository refused an anonymous read); environment variables (`COPILOT_ALLOW_ALL`, `GOOSE_MODE`, `PR_AGENT_CONFIG_BRANCH`); `-dy=1`, `--auto-approve=` and argparse subparser beginnings, wider than the tools (in #264).
- ☑ **2d.5c2c Options built from variables in code in another language** (core; #81, closed; from the fourth review of 2d.5c2a, F-272). Merged as [#266](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/266) by run `wheatear` on 2026-11-19 (merge `e1cecd8`; decisions F-282 to F-284; D-095 in `DECISIONS.md`, proposed). *What changed:* `src/obaaf/checker/agent_code.py` tokenizes Python, JavaScript, Ruby, Perl, PHP, awk and Lua (strings, escapes, interpolation, heredocs, `qw`, `%w`, here-strings, regular expressions, comments) and reads argument lists, command lines in strings (as bash, with every value put in outside the string's quotes), pasted values, names given values, lists in names with their changes and loops; PowerShell and cmd are read word by word; `agent_flags._interpreter` reads `php -r`, options per language, piped code and the program among a script's own arguments. *Stated, not read:* data flow through functions, objects, destructuring and templates held in names; a program name given by escapes, pieces or a run-time path; a second `-e`; a shell the checker does not know beyond a line that starts with the program; AppleScript and Tcl beyond the widest reading. *How it was checked:* `tests/test_agent_code.py` with the reviewers' 802 probes as cases (`tests/agent_code_review_cases.py`), each difference from what a reviewer wanted with its reason; full suite; this repository and Expectrail byte-identical; CI green. The original *done when* follows. *Done when* code given to an interpreter (`python3 -c`, a heredoc fed to `node`, `perl -e`, `ruby -e`, awk), a github-script script and another shell (pwsh, cmd) are read well enough that an argument array held in a variable (`exec.exec('claude', flags)`), a shell string joined with a variable (`os.system("claude " + os.environ["F"])`, `#{ENV["F"]}`, `$ENV{F}`) and an option built from pieces (`"--" + "dangerously-skip-permissions"`, `"-" + "y"`) are "cannot tell", while a literal argument list (`subprocess.run(["claude", "--version"])`) and a single variable in a prompt's place stay quiet; *starting points:* reviewer A's cases Z01 to Z05 and reviewer B's last probes, summarised in #255's description; a sub-agent that did not write it has tried to get past it.
- ☑ **2d.6 Signature keys: only the documented ones, and no traceback.** Merged as [#116](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/116) by run `redshank` on 2026-11-13; found by the review of #113 (decision F-67). No decision in `DECISIONS.md`: it makes the code do what the module and `dsse prepare --help` already documented. *What changed:* `PublicKey.load` in `src/obaaf/verify/dsse.py` refuses every key outside ECDSA P-256 and P-384, Ed25519 and RSA (another curve, DSA, DH, Ed448, X25519, X448, SM2, a key the cryptography library cannot load such as a binary curve), with an error naming the file and the kind; `verify` uses SHA-256 for P-256 and SHA-384 for P-384 and refuses any other curve. `obaaf dsse assemble`, `obaaf verify release` and `obaaf verify agent-run` end with status 1 and the message where a binary-curve key ended them in a traceback. Changelog ("Fixed") and a row in `docs/upgrading.md`. *How it was checked:* `tests/test_dsse_keys.py`, 8 tests, five of which fail on the old code; 1166 tests in all (Python 3.13, cryptography 50.0.1). CI green on #116's head `c177ff4` ([run 43391770461](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43391770461)). One review pass by an Opus 5.5 agent that wrote none of it: the code held (every kind above refused by both commands with no traceback; no valid P-256 or P-384 signature refused, 40 keys per curve in DER, raw and base64; 60k mutated keys and 20k load-and-assemble runs raised only `SignatureError` apart from item 1 below); one blocking finding in the upgrade note's wording, fixed in `c177ff4` (wording only). **Not run:** cryptography 46.0.5 (the `keys` extra's floor, which can still load binary curves: there the curve check refuses them, untested); `obaaf verify release` end to end in a test (run by hand by the reviewer). *Left for later:* (1) RSA has no minimum size; below about 496 bits `verify` raises a `ValueError` that does not name the file, and in `verify release` such a key listed first stops an envelope before a good key is tried; (2) malformed `keys:` entries in a release policy (`keys: [a, b]`, `provenance: 5`) end `verify release` in a traceback, older than this slice (for 2d.2); (3) `sign_envelope`, used only by tests, still picks the hash by key size; (4) a DH key prints a `CryptographyDeprecationWarning` before the error, and binary curves will on cryptography 46.
- ☑ **2d.7 Runner labels: a matrix value with a written default.** Merged as [#147](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/147) by run `sandpiper` on 2026-11-14 (decisions F-117, F-118; D-056 in `DECISIONS.md`, proposed). *What was built:* `Job.runner_label_sets` reads a `runs-on` written as one expression from its tokens and resolves `matrix.x` and `matrix.x || 'label'` against a matrix written out in the file, the default counting wherever a row may lack the value or give an empty one, so the labels are a superset of what the job runs on. Unresolved, in the plain form too: computed values of `x`, a computed `include` or `include` entry, a key in another case, any key that is not plain ASCII text with no expression, a value YAML 1.2 could read as other than text, a YAML merge key anywhere in the file, and any other expression. The old reading resolved two cases wrongly (a computed key beside `include` entries; a computed `include`), and the hosted-label pattern's `$` matched before a final line break (now `\Z`, in the checker and the attestation record). *Result:* on `pallets/flask` at `49943b5`, one medium OBAAF-GHA-013 fewer; on this repository, its fixtures and templates, and Expectrail at `13e8f70`, no change. *How it was checked:* 1401 tests locally on Python 3.13 (1392 before); nine checks green on the merged head `06f66ea` ([run 43558882376](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43558882376)); old against new on 27 targets. *The review:* four passes by one Opus 5.5 sub-agent that wrote none of it, about 50 probes. The first found a key the lookup skipped (`True:`, a computed key, `ſ`), which let the default fill in and GitHub's native firewall control verify for a job that may run self-hosted; the second, a YAML merge key doing the same; both fixed and tested. The third and fourth found nothing blocking; their notes are applied or are issue #148 (three egress runner checks compare labels after `strip()`, older than the slice). *Not run:* a real workflow on GitHub; GitHub's runner source was not read, so how it names a key `True:` is not established (the code refuses it either way). *The specification was:* Found by the review of 3.1 (#140) on the workflow files of `pallets/flask` at `49943b5`, issue [#141](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/issues/141); it is the runner-label part of 2d.2's starting points ("runner labels still match expression text rather than tokens"), taken on its own because it fails a stranger's first gate (decision F-117). `runs-on: ${{ matrix.os || 'ubuntu-latest' }}`, where every value the matrix gives is a GitHub-hosted label, is reported as OBAAF-GHA-013 (medium) because the checker resolves only `${{ matrix.x }}`. *Done when* `runs-on` written as one expression of the form `matrix.x` or `matrix.x || '<label>'` is resolved from a matrix written out in the file, from the expression's tokens, to every label it can take (the default included wherever a row may lack the value); a matrix key, an `include` or an `include` entry computed when the job runs, or a key that differs only in case, leaves it unresolved ("unknown", as now); tests cover Flask's shape and each way it stays unresolved; and which findings change on this repository, its fixtures and Expectrail is recorded. *Needs:* nothing. *Core:* yes.
- ☑ **2d.8 Files the checker could not read, and `--lanes` options.** Merged as [#159](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/159) by run `knot` on 2026-11-14, from #155 (F-125); decision D-059 in `DECISIONS.md` (proposed); the changelog section is "Files the checker could not read". *What changed:* a `CODEOWNERS` file, or a place GitHub looks for one, that cannot be read is an error for that file in `obaaf check` and `--lanes` (it was a traceback, or 0 from `--lanes`), and OBAAF-REPO-001 and 002 wait for it; an agent instruction file that cannot be read is an error (it was skipped, so OBAAF-INS-001 passed it); a directory that cannot be listed or entered is a note (it was a traceback when it could be listed and not entered); `--lanes` refuses before reading anything the options `obaaf check` refuses, and `--write-baseline` and `--output`, with status 2. *Review:* two passes by an Opus 5.5 sub-agent, which ran the permission cases for real as a non-root user on Python 3.11 to 3.13: five blocking, then none. *Checked:* 1460 tests locally; CI green on `70bd472` ([run 43588592319](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43588592319)). *Left, as before and stated:* a `CODEOWNERS` link that leads to no file is no file; instruction files that are links are never looked at (#160); `--lanes` now walks the repository and is slower on a large one. *Not run:* Windows; Python 3.14, where the reviewer believes `Path.is_file` returns False on a permission error instead of raising (not checked), which would turn the error for an unenterable `docs/` into OBAAF-REPO-001.
- ☑ **2d.9 GitHub's firewall runner is a GitHub-hosted label** ([#172](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/issues/172), found by the review of 4.2). Merged as [#174](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/174) by run `phalarope` on 2026-11-15 (decisions F-145, F-146; D-064 in `DECISIONS.md`, proposed). *What was built:* `ubuntu-24.04-firewall` is in `HOSTED_LABELS`, so OBAAF-GHA-013 no longer reports it at medium as a label it cannot name while OBAAF-GHA-020 clears on it, and every other reader of the runner's kind treats it as it treats `ubuntu-latest` (the default shell, `obaaf verify`'s agent-run gate, the runtime selector, Harden-Runner in a container, the settings audit); release 0.3.0 took it for GitHub-hosted too. OBAAF-GHA-020's firewall reading goes through `is_hosted_label` (any letter case, no space trimmed, the job's one label), so a label with spaces round it fails closed in both rules. From the review: a `runs-on` group named `0`, `false`, `''` or null is a group (it was dropped, an old way past); group names are shown as YAML values in findings and facts, and a map with keys of mixed types does not end in a traceback; the build record's isolation tier (`obaaf attest build-record`) reads the runner as the checker does instead of its own `ubuntu-*` pattern, so `ubuntu-gpu` and `[ubuntu-latest, ubuntu-gpu]`, which were recorded as Tier A (able to meet SLSA Build L3), must declare `OBAAF_TIER`, and a matrix of hosted labels is Tier A. The two site sentences that said the development checker still reports OBAAF-GHA-013 for the label were corrected (F-145). Tests: `tests/test_firewall_runner_label.py` (10) and two in `tests/test_fr04_release_isolation.py`; the catalogue, the changelog, four rows of [upgrading.md](upgrading.md). *Review:* one Opus 5.5 sub-agent, seven passes (0, 0, 1, 0, 2, 1 blocking, then a one-line example applied as worded); 69 bypass attempts on the label, no spelling on which the two rules disagree; the ways past were the false-looking group (fixed) and `{Group: …}` in another case, which GitHub probably refuses (not verified). *Run:* 1604 tests (Python 3.13); `obaaf catalog`, `obaaf docs build --check`, `obaaf check . --fail-on medium`, `python site/build.py` exit 0; #172's repository with CODEOWNERS exits 0 where it exited 1; old and new checker on a copy of Expectrail's `.github` at `ebadc25`: the same 43 findings; CI green on `8048f28` ([run 43676191530](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43676191530)). *Not run or not established:* GitHub's handling of the preview label on a self-hosted runner (an account outside the preview might give a self-hosted runner that label; the catalogue says the label is taken at its word), of a label with spaces round it, of `group: 0` or `false`, and of mapping keys in another case. *Left:* `HOSTED_LABEL_RE` in `workflow.py` is unused since 2d.2d. *Done when* a job on `ubuntu-24.04-firewall` with an enforcing policy and nothing else against it passes `--fail-on medium`; OBAAF-GHA-013 and OBAAF-GHA-020 read the label the same way, with tests; and the catalogue, the changelog, the upgrading note and the site say so.
- ☑ **2d.10 A GitHub-hosted label written twice is that label** ([#216](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/issues/216), found by the review of 4.7). Built by run `dunnock` (F-218); merged as [#220](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/220) by run `redpoll` on 2026-11-18 (merge `53e739c`) after `dunnock`'s session stalled and its claim lapsed (F-219, F-229); decision D-080 in `DECISIONS.md` (proposed; it amends D-064). *What changed:* `Job.runner_kind` reads `[ubuntu-latest, ubuntu-latest]`, or one GitHub-hosted label in two letter cases, as that label, as the native firewall's reading has since 2d.2f; OBAAF-GHA-013, `obaaf verify`'s agent-run gate, the build record's tier, Harden-Runner's container check and the settings audit follow. *How it was checked:* six tests that failed on `6a14758`; 2021 tests locally, by `dunnock` and again by `redpoll`; CI green on `0f15a07` (11 of 11, run 44195598668); three review passes (3, 3 and 0 blocking). *Not read:* GitHub's page on how labels are matched.

**2e. No breadth for its own sake.** Standing rule, not a slice: depth on GitHub Actions first. Jenkins stays as it is (advisory); no other CI system is added. From the positioning (1.4, decision F-19): no new general workflow rule is added either; general weaknesses are zizmor's, CodeQL's, actionlint's and poutine's to report.

### 3. The easiest adoption path

#### P0 adoption implementation

The lead reserves every row. The approved [adoption design](adoption-experience-design.md) specifies command behavior and the [tracking issue](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/issues/51) records claims. Each implementation PR tests the normal CLI, records before and after on OBAAF and a read-only copy of Expectrail, and proposes a D-numbered decision for the behavior introduced. The tools run offline with the standard library and PyYAML. Keep finding identity, severity and report detail except for the approved GHA-005 lifecycle change.

| Slice | Needs | Done when |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A1a Cause catalogue ([#252](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/issues/252)) | #251 (done). Done: merged as [#253](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/253) (`2f99a99`) by run `brambling`, #252 closed | Each rule/kind belongs to exactly one cause with an ID, sentence, fix kind and next command. Catalogue tests reject gaps and duplicates. |
| A1b Grouping and upgrade pairs (#257, PR #258) | A1a (done). Done: merged as [#258](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/258) (`5316c64`) by run `brambling`, #257 closed | Uncounted acceptances group first by reason, then other outstanding findings by rule/kind. Accepted and cleared findings are excluded. Tests cover unique membership, counts by severity and display-only stale matches by rule, file and job. JSON adds `todo` and preserves existing fields. Changeable tool versions have one entry with separate counts for candidate pins and manual work, as Sean confirmed. |
| A1c Report and summary (#259) | A1b | The list appears immediately before the version. Fixable causes come first, then severity and count. Commands preserve the check options. Test full text, `--summary`, unchanged SARIF and the command to accept remaining risk with counts by severity. |
| A2a Private-copy proof (#261) | A1b | Apply candidate edits outside the repository and run the same check with the same path, config, baseline, platform and threshold. Tests require exactly the promised finding changes, no newly counting finding or stale entry, only the intended YAML keys changed, and no other file difference. Report why a candidate failed. |
| A2b Paperwork preview | A2a | List every acceptance separately with the user-supplied owner and, for migration, reason and expiry. Cover ownerless ignores, old baselines, unique one-edit lane corrections and individually listed stale removals. Tests cover ambiguity and withholding an edit that disturbs another acceptance. Print missing CODEOWNERS lines for human approval. |
| A2c Apply reviewed edits | A2b | Preview prints a unified diff. Explicit `--apply` recomputes and reproves it, then writes precisely that diff. Dirty targets stop all writes. Tests verify partial failure handling and a repeated run. Existing bulk acceptance stays a separate explicit command. |
| A3a Egress draft | A2a | Using a test catalogue, rewrite one job’s agent step as a draft, prove its predicted verdicts on a private copy and print each unmet condition. Cover supported and other provider keys, jobs needing a split and missing listed pins. The actual job stays untouched. |
| A3b Egress release verification | A3a and Sean’s approval on #248 | With the approved commit listed, verify the real command prints its draft, control outcomes, host discovery guidance and remaining limits. Before listing, the released-catalogue path explains the hold. |
| A4a Offline settings evidence | A2a | Coordinate interfaces with the GitHub builder. Prepare and test the reader independently. Production GHA-005 verdicts and clearing stay as they are until A4b.  Read only an explicit snapshot. Test completeness of environment, repository and organisation secret names and branch rules, date reporting, the 30-day boundary, and reasons for unread settings. Require code ownership and source snapshot data from the PR target branch. |
| A4b Clear GHA-005 | A4a, core slot | Verify the second control only on complete, fresh evidence. End-to-end tests clear the finding when all five verify, preserve it otherwise, and check the report and snapshot date. |
| A5a Checkout tightening | A2c | On explicit request by kind, preview and prove the one-line GHA-011 credential change. Test a held-back edit affecting an acceptance and an explicit request by name. |
| A5b Tool install tightening | A2c | On request, apply the user’s exact package version to eligible GHA-025 installs after proof and explicit apply. Test unsupported forms and missing versions as manual work. |
| A6a Work order | A3a | Test `--brief` with a test catalogue. It labels quoted, length-limited repository names as data, directs generation from a trusted branch, specifies the proof command and forbids widening acceptances. |
| A6b Work order verification | A6a, A3b | Verify the work order with the approved production pin and report the real checker verdict. Sean’s #248 decision gates completion. |
| A7a Init handoff and gate switch | 3.3, A1c, A2c | Init ends with the list and a pointer to fix. Test the explicit `--gate LEVEL` preview and apply on init’s workflow, target-branch baseline and configuration on PRs, and manual guidance for custom CI. |
| A7b Adoption documentation | A1–A6, A7a | Walk through report-only CI, reviewed fixes, explicit acceptance and gate activation. Verify the commands and explain manual work, confinement limits and what the settings snapshot proves. |
| 3.6 Timed trial (step 7) | A7b | A stranger uses a real agent repository and records times and stumbles from install to useful results (under five minutes) and a CI gate blocking only new risk (under fifteen). Hand input is limited to decisions and items marked manual. |

- ☑ **3.1 Measure what exists.** Merged as [#140](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/140), a ChatGPT session's page published from Sean's session (`whimbrel`), reviewed and merged as `review-gpt` by run `sandpiper` on 2026-11-14 (decisions F-109 to F-111). *What it adds:* [adoption-path-measured.md](adoption-path-measured.md), listed in the docs index: one run of the released 0.3.0 (`pip install obaaf` on Python 3.12.14, Linux x86_64, pip's cache warm) from installation to a gate file, on a minimal one-workflow layout and on the five workflow files of `pallets/flask` at `49943b5`, with every command, its exit status and output. *Times* (summed command time, not a person's time): to the first useful report 13.8 s on each layout, to the gate file checked locally 14.6 s and 14.7 s, about 94% of it `pip install`. **No real CI run was made**: the gate is timed up to the workflow file being written and its shape checked. *Stumbles:* the baseline step of [from-report-to-gate.md](from-report-to-gate.md) needs `--owner`, `--reason` and `--expires`, which 0.3.0 does not have (that page says so on its line 5); `obaaf templates install` after `pip install` fails, because the package does not ship the templates, while the README and the adoption guide say to run it ([#142](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/issues/142)); the CI workflow had to be written by hand; and, found by the review, a false OBAAF-GHA-013 on Flask's `runs-on: ${{ matrix.os || 'ubuntu-latest' }}`, where every matrix value is a GitHub-hosted label, fails a medium gate ([#141](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/issues/141)). *The review:* the reviewing run rebuilt both layouts from the page's text, built 0.3.0 as a wheel from its tag (PyPI is unreachable from the scheduled runs) and got every exit status and output block the page shows; it checked every cited line at `9acb761`. Two passes by an Opus 5.5 sub-agent that wrote neither the page nor the reviewer's additions: the first found two blocking problems (the page put the missing-owner finding down to copying only the workflows, but Flask has no CODEOWNERS file at that commit and 0.3.0 gives the same seven findings on its whole tree; and stale file counts in the pull request's description) and seven smaller ones; the second, nothing blocking in the page. All are corrected in the page or in #142, with a dated section saying what the reviewing run checked and changed. *Not run or not checked:* the times (they come from the ChatGPT session's logs, in the handover archive, not in this repository; they add up), `pip install` from PyPI, a real CI run, a person's time. Slice 3.6 measures the path again once 3.2 to 3.4 exist.
- ☑ **3.2 Zero-config first run.** Done on 2026-11-16, when 3.2b was merged (#200, run `scaup`). *Done when* `obaaf check` with no configuration prints a summary a stranger can act on: each finding, why it matters, the exact fix. *Positioning (1.4):* the summary also names what OBAAF does not check and which tools do (zizmor, actionlint, poutine, GitHub's default code scanning setup), in one or two lines, so a stranger running only OBAAF knows what they are missing. *Split on 2026-11-14 by run `stint` (decision F-123) into 3.2a and 3.2b; 3.2 is done when both are.*
  - ☑ **3.2a The first run says what it did not read.** Merged as [#154](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/154) by run `stint` on 2026-11-14; decision D-057 is proposed. *What changed:* `obaaf check --lanes` reports as an `error:` line everything `obaaf check` reports as an error for a workflow or Jenkinsfile (a file it could not read or parse, an input given twice, an expression too deep; it now runs the workflow rules to find them) and ends with 1, a path with no workflow or Jenkinsfile with 2, and a Jenkinsfile part of whose pipeline was not analyzed is a note, as for `check`; before, it ended 0 in silence in every case. The text report ends with `Checked by obaaf VERSION.`; with no configuration file, two lines before it say an agent job is recognised only from a known tool or `OBAAF_LANE: agent`, and that zizmor, actionlint, poutine and code scanning's default setup find what OBAAF does not look for. `obaaf check` no longer ends in a traceback on a workflow file it cannot read (older). *How it was checked:* 1430 tests locally (14 new in `tests/test_first_run.py`); CI green on `a480c6e` ([run 43572568091](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43572568091)); Expectrail at `8083215` on a copy: `--lanes` and the gate unchanged but for the version line; the reviewer compared `--lanes` and `check` statuses on 100 targets with every `--platform`: no mismatch. *Review:* two passes by an Opus 5.5 sub-agent: three blocking in the first (the first-run line untrue for `OBAAF_LANE`; a partly analyzed Jenkinsfile; an expression too deep ended `--lanes` 0), one in the second (a `--config` phrase untrue for a root file, fixed in wording only). *Left:* #155 (an unreadable CODEOWNERS still ends `check` in a traceback; `--lanes` ignores options that make `check` end 2); 3.2b. *From the plan (the specification it was built to):* `obaaf check --lanes` reports a workflow or Jenkinsfile it could not read as an error and ends with 1, and a path with nothing to list or not there with 2; the text report names the version that wrote it; a text report with no configuration file says that agent jobs were recognised only from known tools and names what OBAAF does not check and which tools do. *Done when* each is tested and documented in the changelog, `upgrading.md` and a Proposed decision.
  - ☑ **3.2b Why each finding matters.** Merged as [#200](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/200) on 2026-11-16, built by run `eider` and, after its claim lapsed, reviewed again, corrected and merged by run `scaup` (F-184, F-185); decision D-073 is proposed. *What changed:* every one of the 43 rules of `obaaf check` has a `why` in `src/obaaf/data/rules.yaml`, one sentence of at most 160 characters that `obaaf catalog` checks; the text report prints it as `Why:` under each finding's message, `--no-fixes` leaves it out with the `Fix:` line (so the first adopter's gate prints the same report), JSON has `why` on each finding, SARIF's rule help begins with it and the generated rule page has it. `--write-baseline` still leaves out a finding an `ignore` entry names without counting and one no entry can accept, now lists each as a `not written:` line on standard error (with the finding's message where no entry can accept it, which names the files with no owner), counts the file it writes among the files that need a code owner, and ends with 1 when one of them is at or above `--fail-on`, as the gate reading the new baseline would; below it, a warning and 0. *How it was checked:* 1847 tests locally on Python 3.13.18 (1839 before; `tests/test_why_it_matters.py` is new); `obaaf catalog`, `obaaf docs build --check` and `obaaf check . --fail-on medium` exit 0; CI green on `e169950` (run 43930709381); Expectrail at `ada4c34`, on a copy without `fresh-*`, with its CI command: standard output and standard error byte-identical before and after (exit 1 both times, 44 new findings that predate the slice), 44 `Why:` lines without `--no-fixes`; `--write-baseline` on that copy writes 39 findings and ends with 1, with 5 `not written:` lines for its `ignore` entries that name no owner, as the gate then does. *Review:* three passes by Opus 5.5 sub-agents that did not write it. The first (run `eider`'s) found that the baseline being written must count as a gate file and eight sentences that said too much. The second (run `scaup`'s) built a fixture for each kind of finding and compared `--write-baseline` with its gate for ten `--fail-on` forms on five fixtures and Expectrail (no disagreement), and found four blocking sentences, OBAAF-GHA-010, 019, JEN-006 and 009, which stated as certain a consequence some of their own findings contradict, and seven premises that did not fit every kind; ten sentences and the descriptions of GHA-019 and JEN-006 were corrected. The third found nothing blocking; its wording notes were applied. *Left:* the settings audit's rules have no `why` (#202); OBAAF-GHA-017 does not count `npm publish --provenance` (#201, a false positive older than this slice); the website shows release 0.3.0's output, without the line, until a release has it; `--no-fixes` hides the line, so the first adopter's gate does not show it (D-073 chose this; Sean to confirm, item 51 of [Waiting on Sean](#waiting-on-sean)).
  - ☑ **3.2c The settings audit's rules say why a finding matters.** Merged as [#211](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/211) by run `pintail` on 2026-11-17 (merge `fbb0b96`), added under F-204 from #202, which 3.2b left; decision D-077 in `DECISIONS.md` is proposed; plan decisions F-204 to F-206. *What changed:* each rule of `obaaf audit`, `OBAAF-SET-001` to `015`, has a `why` in `src/obaaf/audit/rules.py`, a required keyword field of `Rule`: one sentence of at most 160 characters, held to the catalogue's sentence test (its pattern and limit, imported by `tests/test_audit_why.py`). The text report prints it as `Why:` once per rule, under its findings and just before its `Fix:`; `--no-fixes` leaves out both and its help says so; a rule with only "cannot tell" answers gets neither (F-205). Each JSON entry under `rules` gains `why` and `fix`. `docs/settings-audit.md` lists the fifteen sentences (a test keeps them equal, with nothing stale) and its example has the line. *Review:* three passes by one Opus 5.5 sub-agent that did not write it, with a fixture for each kind of finding it doubted: 5 blocking findings, then none, then none. The five were sentences that stated as certain what some of their rule's findings contradict: `OBAAF-SET-002` in a private repository, `003` with no environment, `005` beside Copilot's review, `008` beside another rule a merge waits for, and `011` where the called job declares the environment. Five more did not fit some kind, and all were reworded; the review also caught a test that matched the six-space `Why:` lines about settings not read. *Run:* 1964 tests locally (12 new); CI green on the head `b5a0e44` ([run 44034127305](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/44034127305)). Before and after, the audit's reports from saved snapshots differ only by the new lines and keys: this repository (0 findings, text byte-identical) and Expectrail at `3b42a95` without `fresh-*` (9 findings, three `Why:` lines added). *Not run:* a live audit with a token; GitHub's pages (no network). *Left:* #212, two older faults the review found (`OBAAF-SET-011` by name when the called job declares the environment; `OBAAF-SET-001` and `002` beside custom deployment protection rules); drift lines get no sentence; the site does not show the audit's output.
- ☐ **3.3 `obaaf init`.** *Done when* it inspects a repository, proposes `.github/obaaf.yml` (including detected agent jobs and secrets) and adds a workflow, with no file written by hand. *Positioning (1.4):* it proposes agent jobs from the named tools and also from any job that holds a model provider's key (most of Expectrail's agent jobs have no named tool), and it offers to add pinned zizmor, actionlint and poutine steps beside OBAAF, off unless accepted and reporting only (zizmor reported 85 findings on Expectrail, 82 of them low, so a gate on day one would be noise). For a team that has no agent workflow yet, it points to GitHub Agentic Workflows instead of installing OBAAF's agent lane templates (decision F-21). *From decision F-51:* F-21 is confirmed, and Sean's reason was easier onboarding, so the path this slice offers a team with no agent workflow must be shorter than assembling the templates by hand, and the timing in 3.6 should show it. Sean chose preview then explicit apply, stopping before either write on a conflict, reporting findings in the first CI workflow, and listing ambiguous secret names for review (#84, decision record #249).
- ☑ **3.4 CI integration.** Merged as [#149](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/149): a ChatGPT session's handover, reviewed, corrected and merged as `review-gpt` by run `stint` on 2026-11-14 (decisions F-121 and F-122). No decision in `DECISIONS.md`: no checker code changed. *What it adds:* [ci-integration.md](ci-integration.md), linked from the docs index, the adoption guide (beside CodeQL's default setup) and [from-report-to-gate.md](from-report-to-gate.md) (3.5's note). It says what to pin (the v0.3.0 commit `5162a17` and what it lacks, or the example's `a620b95`, labelled as not a release); the exit status, as in from-report-to-gate.md; what JSON and SARIF carry and where; that code scanning does not show an unread file, so the gate step must stay able to fail; a required check whose workflow is the template `obaaf-pipeline-check.yml` word for word with its pin filled in; a local pre-commit hook that checks the working tree; what was run and what is still missing. **The template changed with it** (F-121): it read the target branch's configuration only at `.github/obaaf.yml`, so a repository with `.github/obaaf.yaml` or `obaaf.yml` had its pull requests checked with none; it now sparse-checks-out all three and takes the first, as the checker does, decides "pull request" from `GITHUB_EVENT_NAME`, removes a `.obaaf-base` the pull request brings, fails when the configuration it would read is a link, and runs on `edited`. *Tests:* `tests/test_ci_integration.py`, 15 tests: report parity on a failing gate, status 1 and a `not-read` SARIF error for an unread file, status 2 for nothing to check and a misplaced `--format`, the page's workflow equal to the template and free of findings, every `obaaf` command on the page parsed and every `--fail-on` value checked, the failing example run as written, and the template's check step run with bash and a stand-in for `obaaf`. *What the review found in the handover:* its workflow read the configuration from the pull request's own revision (a pull request could accept its own findings); its `upload-sarif` pin could not be tied to a release (the template's v4.38.2 is used); its exit table was observations, not the contract. *Review:* four passes by one Opus 5.5 sub-agent: four blocking, then two, then none, then none (F-122). *How it was checked:* 1416 tests locally on Python 3.13; CI green on the merged head `c19712e`, actionlint on the template included ([run 43567890411](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43567890411)). **Not run:** the workflow on a GitHub runner, an upload to code scanning, a required check in a ruleset, pre-commit in the reviewing session (no PyPI; the ChatGPT session ran pre-commit 4.7.0), actionlint locally. *Left:* an official `.pre-commit-hooks.yaml`, and whether it may resolve dependencies at install time (#151); no promise about the JSON and SARIF formats (`obaaf-check/v0.1` did not change when `baseline.stale` entries did); the old bash on macOS and `.obaaf-base` contents a pull request brings, both older than the slice and not changed. *From the plan (the specification it was built to):* An official pinned GitHub Action, SARIF for code scanning, a pre-commit hook, stable exit codes and JSON output. *Done when* each is documented and tested; what exists today (`actions/setup-obaaf`, `--format sarif`, `--format json`) is checked before anything is built. The adoption guide says to turn on GitHub's default code scanning setup; OBAAF's SARIF goes to the same place. The SARIF gaps noted by 2d.1 and 2d.2a are closed in the code since 2a.3 (errors and notes are notifications of the run), and the page says code scanning does not show them; a pre-commit hook is given the whole working tree, so a Jenkinsfile and the `lanes:` entries are read as in CI.
- ☑ **3.5 Versioning and moving from report-only to gate.** Merged as [#110](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/110) by run `sanderling` (the same session as `dunlin`). No code of the checker changed. *What it adds:* [upgrading.md](upgrading.md): what to pin, how to move to a newer OBAAF in a pull request of its own, and for 0.3.0 to the code on `main` a table of everything that can turn a passing gate red with what to do about each, what a script that reads the output will notice, one upgrade from start to finish with the lines the tool prints, and Expectrail's three steps. [from-report-to-gate.md](from-report-to-gate.md): the path from reading the report to a check that fails a build, one command and one exit status at a time. `tests/test_adoption_path.py` runs both sequences on the small repository in `tests/fixtures/adoption`, whose baseline was written by the real release 0.3.0, and parses every `obaaf` command in the two pages' code blocks with the command line's own parser. The upgrade section is a living one (decision F-60). *How it was checked:* 949 tests locally on Python 3.13 (939 before; 10 new); nine checks green on the merged head `a4b8753`, Python 3.10 among them ([run 43326544870](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43326544870)). The upgrade sequence was first run by hand with release 0.3.0, extracted from its tag, and then with this code on the same files: with 0.3.0 the gate passes with "4 finding(s) accepted by the baseline, 0 new"; with this code it ends with status 1 and "0 accepted, 5 new", and the page's steps bring it back to status 0 with 7 accepted. Expectrail at `776ddd3`, on a copy: 40 findings count with its own command, and 38 are accepted and 2 new after `obaaf baseline migrate` on a copy of its baseline. *The review, two passes by agents that did not write it:* the first (16 minutes) ran every sentence against the tool and against 0.3.0 and found **seven untrue sentences while the test passed**: `--lanes` ends with 0 and says nothing of a workflow it could not read; pinning the action of an unnamed step makes an accepted finding on it new, and writing the baseline again accepts it under a new owner and date; JSON does not keep every field (the entries under `baseline.stale` changed); a time of day in `until` was a traceback in 0.3.0 and not a passing gate; the text report names no version; step 4 can end with 0 and leave out a finding whose ignore entry does not count; and the page claimed the test kept it from drifting. The second (6 minutes), on the fourteen rewritten sentences, found three that still said more than the tool does and one wrong at its edge. All were reworded; after the second pass only words changed, and no test (decision F-61). **Not run:** `pip install obaaf`, the `setup-obaaf` action, a real CI run of the gate (the session cannot reach PyPI; the pages describe commands, not installation); the times the path takes (3.1, 3.6); an upload to code scanning; baselines written by 0.2.0; Jenkins. *What the review found in the tool, not in the pages, each older than this slice and none fixed here because the slice changes no code:* (1) **`obaaf check --lanes` ends with 0 and prints nothing about a workflow it could not read, a directory with no workflows, or a path that does not exist.** It is the command a team uses to confirm that every agent job is seen, so an agent job in a workflow that does not parse is silently missing from the list. (2) **A step with no `name` or `id` is identified by the action it uses, version included, so the fix OBAAF itself recommends for OBAAF-GHA-008 (pin the action) makes every other accepted finding on that step new**, and `--write-baseline` then accepts it again under whatever owner and date are given. (3) The text report does not say which version wrote it. (4) `--write-baseline` ends with 0 while leaving out a finding whose ignore entry does not count; it warns on standard error only. *For 2d.2:* (1) and (2) above; for (2), identify an unnamed step by its action without the ref, or by position, and decide it with the reviewer. *For 3.2 (first run):* (1), (3) and (4): `--lanes` should fail or at least say when a file was not read; the first lines of the text report could name the version. *For 3.4:* when the action and the pre-commit hook exist, the path page gets a step that installs them; today it has none. *For 3.6:* time the path on this page; its commands carry example dates that will pass (2027-01-31 and 2027-03-31), which no test notices. Two small things in the test, left because nothing reviewed test code after the second pass: `test_a_baseline_written_before_the_lanes_are_right_accepts_the_wrong_declaration` has one assertion that cannot fail (`assertNotIn("OBAAF-GHA-003", out)`), and `test_one_upgrade_start_to_finish` passes an older reason text than the page's command. *For every later slice that changes what passes or what a report says:* add it to `docs/upgrading.md` in the slice's own pull request, where its reviewer sees it (decision F-60); the "For 3.5" notes under 2a.3 and 2a.4 are in the page. *Done when* there is an upgrade note and a documented path from reporting to gating. *From 2a.1:* the lifecycle change breaks a passing gate on purpose (decisions F-38 and F-39): a v0.1 baseline and an `ignore` entry with no owner stop accepting. The upgrade note is at the top of the changelog's "Unreleased" section and in [checker-configuration.md](checker-configuration.md#baseline-migrate); this slice should make it the note for the version that carries it, and should say what Expectrail has to do when it moves its pin: run `obaaf baseline migrate` and add `owner:` to its one ignore entry, both in a `needs-sean` pull request there, since they touch the baseline. *From #102:* the version scheme is settled (decision F-44) and is in [releasing.md](releasing.md); what is left for this slice is the upgrade note and the path from reporting to gating.
- ☐ **3.6 Timed again.** *Done when* a stranger's path is under five minutes to a useful result and under fifteen to a gate in CI, measured, with the times and stumbles recorded.
- ☑ **3.7 Expectrail case study.** Merged as [#126](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/126) by run `kittiwake` on 2026-11-13 (decision F-86). No decision in `DECISIONS.md`: nothing an adopter runs changed. *What was built:* [expectrail-case-study.md](expectrail-case-study.md), linked from the docs index and from [expectrail-settings-audit.md](expectrail-settings-audit.md). It opens by saying that Expectrail is a project of one of OBAAF's two authors and that results from outside teams are still to come. Sources: Expectrail's repository and full history, OBAAF's repository and pull requests, and GitHub's answers to a caller with no token; the six incidents come from the brief, as published in the settings audit's guide, and the page shows what the public record supports for each (Expectrail's commit messages and its `docs/obaaf.md`). It covers how Expectrail uses OBAAF (Phase A on 2026-10-30, the gate on `main` from #223 at 17:36 UTC on 2026-11-10, pinned to `d014b63`), what worked (declared agent jobs: 2 of 19 recognised unaided; keys named in `main-only`; the gate caught `jit.yml#propose` about three hours after it reached `main`, and the findings were accepted), what did not (the baseline went 32, 34, 36, 38 in four commits, each adding one new job's GHA-005 and GHA-020, none with an owner, reason or expiry; the incidents, as reported, were in settings; nothing enforced; the checker was young, #50 and `1df61fb`; the `probe` label), what has changed since on `flagship`, what Expectrail would see on moving its pin, and what the page does not show. *What was run:* on Expectrail at `da063233`, copied out with `git archive`: the pinned gate (OBAAF `d014b63`: 38 accepted, 0 new, exit 0); the same gate with OBAAF at `7fb8cac` (40 new, 20 high and 20 low, exit 1); after `obaaf baseline migrate` and an `owner:` on the ignore entry, on a copy (39 accepted, 1 new: `OBAAF-CFG-001`, exit 1); `--lanes` without the configuration (2 agent jobs); the baseline at each commit that touched it; `obaaf audit` on the snapshot of 09:10 UTC with the files at `da06323` (4 medium, 7 "cannot tell", as at `36a7a8a`). A live audit of Expectrail from the session was refused by its proxy (the repository is in scope as `seanudeke/expectrail`, and GitHub's redirect goes to a numeric path the proxy refuses). Nine checks green on the merged head `aa22281` ([CI 43494299051](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43494299051)); 1260 tests and `obaaf docs build --check` locally. *Fact-check:* two passes by one Opus 5.5 sub-agent that wrote none of it, which re-ran every number and read every source. The first found 11 blocking problems, among them: the pin's date; a gate said to have "stopped" a job it did not stop; "no fix Expectrail could apply", false because a blocking Harden-Runner step cleared GHA-020 at the pin; the unaided detection misdescribed (ten of the other 17 install an agent tool with `curl … | bash`); GHA-024's reach; incidents stated as fact; `AGENTS.md` misquoted; one author named instead of two. The second found two, both wording. Changes after it are wording only. *For Sean, as information:* Expectrail's `docs/obaaf.md` still describes "Protect main" as requiring one approval and a passing `test` check; GitHub's answer of 2026-11-13 shows 0 approvals and 12 required checks. Nothing was changed there. *From the plan (the specification it was built to):* From public facts only, with numbers recomputed here, stating plainly that Expectrail is the author's own project and that outside results are still to come.
- ☑ **3.8 "Run this and send us the result".** Merged as [#205](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/205) by run `wigeon` on 2026-11-16 (merge `70344fb`); decision D-074 is proposed; plan decisions F-187 to F-192. *What was built:* `python -m obaaf.result PATH --output FILE` (`src/obaaf/result.py`) runs `obaaf check` in the same process with the `--config`, `--baseline`, `--fail-on` and `--platform` it is given and writes `obaaf-result/v0.1`: findings by rule, severity and state; controls by rule, control and verdict; accepted risk with the number of owners; files checked and not read; notes; jobs and Jenkins stages in each lane with the configuration and without it; the exit status and the time. With `--audit`, a report from `obaaf audit --format json`, it adds one row per settings rule, each setting's status and reason code, whether a token was used and its roles, and the repository's visibility and owner type. Every key and string is checked against OBAAF's vocabulary before writing, numbers must be below a million, and otherwise nothing is written (exit 2); unknown values count as `other`. The output must be outside the repository being checked. [send-us-the-result.md](send-us-the-result.md) is the page: install the pinned commit `f4ee9b6` with the dependencies hash-checked, on Python 3.10 to 3.14 (`OBAAF_PYTHON` for a newer one), check, optionally audit, write and read the file, what the counts can still tell, five questions to answer in the reply, the template for a finding that changed what was shipped, and clean-up; two issue forms under `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/`; an advisory CI job runs the page's blocks as written on GitHub-hosted Ubuntu and macOS. *How it was checked:* CI green on `5749a21`, all 11 checks ([run 43964961539](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43964961539)): on Ubuntu the runner's Python 3.14.4 installed in 8.6 s and steps 2 to 5 took 5.7 s; on macOS the runner's Python 3.15.2 was refused with the page's message and Python 3.14.10 installed in 9.2 s, steps 2 to 5 in 2.8 s. The first head's macOS job had failed (pip found no PyYAML 6.0.3 for 3.15), which set the range (F-189). 1880 tests locally on Python 3.13 (1847 before; 33 in `tests/test_result_file.py`); `obaaf catalog`, `obaaf docs build --check`, `obaaf check . --fail-on medium`, `python site/build.py` exit 0. Expectrail at `ba050a7`, a copy without `fresh-*`: exit 1, 44 new, 19 agent jobs with its configuration and 2 without. *Review:* four passes by one Opus 5.5 sub-agent that wrote none of it: 1 blocking and 9 non-blocking in the first (the macOS install; `\d` matching other scripts' digits; one unbounded integer carrying a token from an altered audit report; a traceback on deep JSON; `stage …` jobs counted as stages; a hyphen-led option value refused; the counts partly tested; overstated wording; links to forms that only work from `main`; a stale result file sendable after a failed run), 1 blocking in the second (two tests failed on Python 3.10) with three more (removing the old output could delete the found configuration, the checked workflow, a pipe, `/dev/null` as root; an audit's rule list copied row for row), none blocking in the third (a workflows-directory target could still overwrite the configuration; a symlink loop crashed before 3.13; a D-074 sentence) and none in the fourth. Every one was fixed with a test that fails on the commit before it. The reviewer read CI logs with `gh api`, read-only. *Not run:* `pip install` from PyPI in the session (CI ran it), Windows or WSL, a private repository, the audit with a real token, Intel macOS, ARM Linux, Python 3.10 to 3.13 on the page's path, a person's time. *Left:* the command is not yet `obaaf result` (core; F-187); the page pins a commit until a release has the module (F-188); if `$HOME` is a git work tree and the checked directory has none, `~/obaaf-try` is refused as inside the repository, and the message does not name the root it found (the fourth pass, non-blocking); the counts of a report altered by hand can still carry data, bounded, not stopped (F-191); the forms show only once they are on `main`; what the maintainers do with results is for Sean to say (item 53). *From the plan (the specification it was built to):* One page for Sean's contacts, a result file with no secrets and no source code, and a short template for reporting a finding that changed what they shipped.

Merged as [#217](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/217) on 2026-11-17 (merge `16822e6`). *What changed:* the package from PyPI does not include `templates/`, so the command the README and the adoption guide printed exits 2 after `pip install obaaf` (reproduced with 0.3.0 built from its tag and with a non-editable install of `flagship`), and running it inside a clone does not help. `README.md`, `docs/adoption-guide.md`, `templates/github-actions/README.md` and `docs/live-acceptance-tests.md` now clone OBAAF outside the repository being set up (`../obaaf-src` where the install goes to `.`), check out `<commit>`, the SHA the workflows will pin (with `git -C <clone> rev-parse 'v0.3.0^{commit}'` for release 0.3.0), and pass `--source <clone>/templates/github-actions`; each says `OWNER/obaaf` is `Obaaf/OBAAF` or a fork that keeps its name (a renamed fork gets OBAAF-GHA-021 from the development checker), and that an editable install from the clone needs no `--source` (a non-editable one does) (F-216). `tests/test_templates_install_docs.py` (3 tests) reads each page's clone, checkout and every command with an option, requires `--source` to be the clone's templates, `--obaaf-sha` the checked-out commit and the clone outside the repository when `--dest` is `.`, checks each `rev-parse` line (skipped in a shallow checkout without the tag), and runs each command as printed. A changelog entry. *Review:* an Opus 5.5 sub-agent that did not write it, four passes (1, 2, 0 and 0 blocking findings): the changelog said any install from the clone works; the changelog gave one `--source` path for all pages; one commit message (`fdabe7c`) said every page gave the SHA and the fork's name, which the live acceptance tests did not until the next commit (F-217); its mutations of the pages (seven kinds) all fail the test now. *Run:* 2017 tests locally on the merged head (Python 3.13); `obaaf docs build --check`, `obaaf catalog`, `obaaf check . --fail-on medium` exit 0; the reviewer ran every page's commands as printed from inside a scratch repository with 0.3.0 and a non-editable `flagship` install (all exit 0, the clone never inside the reader's repository); CI green on the merged head `5b09eee`, 11 of 11 ([run 44189495904](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/44189495904)). *Not run:* PyPI, Windows, macOS. *Left, core (#142 stays open):* the command's error message ("run from a clone of the OBAAF repository or pass --source", where only `--source` or an editable install works) and its closing line (`templates/github-actions/CODEOWNERS.example` as a path in the current directory), both in `cli.py`; OBAAF-GHA-023's fix text, which still prints the command without `--source`; and shipping the templates in the package, left to 3.3. *Done when* every page that tells a reader to run `obaaf templates install` gives commands that work after `pip install obaaf`, each says where the templates come from, a test holds each page to a working command, and a sub-agent that did not write it has reviewed it. It changes those four pages, the changelog and one new test.

### 4. The website

The site lives in [`site/`](../site/README.md), is built by `site/build.py`, and deploys to GitHub Pages from `main` after CI. That stays unless a slice records a strong reason to change it. Because it deploys only from `main`, slices provide screenshots and build instructions.

*From #102 (decision F-44):* the self-assessment form on the site is built from `main`, so it now names the development version ("OBAAF 0.4.0.dev0, a development version and not a release") beside a front page that names the latest release. It was always the form on `main`; it used to carry the last release's number whatever `main` held. Slice 4.1 decides whether the site, or at least the form, should be built from the latest release instead, and until then the front page's "Version 0.3" is written by hand and has to be changed by hand at each release.

- ☑ **4.1 Content and truth table.** Merged as [#129](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/129), the work of a ChatGPT session published by a Claude session in Sean's session, reviewed and merged as `review-gpt` by run `dotterel` on 2026-11-13 (decision F-95). *What was built:* [site-truth-table.md](site-truth-table.md): 132 current claims, 36 planned claims and 26 numbers on the website, each with a verdict and a publication decision, audited against `e87d376`; a recommendation (not a decision) that the site keep deploying from `main` and that the public assessment be built from a pinned stable release; nothing under `site/` changed. *Review:* the repository's checks on the branch merged with `flagship` (1336 tests, catalogue, docs, `obaaf check`, the site build) and CI on the head `eee0ac6` ([run 43519516974](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43519516974)) passed; an Opus 5.5 sub-agent re-checked every link, extract and number with its own scripts and every Backed security row and number entry, and found nothing blocking; the run added a dated section of rows made out of date by later merges (2c.3, 2c.4, H2, 3.7, the licence) and narrowed C064 and C108; the corrections are in [a comment on #129](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/129#issuecomment-6866537386). *Not checked by the reviewing run:* the released-package and `verify`/`attest`/`proposal` fixture runs (PyPI unreachable), which rest on the handover. 4.2 and 4.3 now have what they need. The original specification follows. *Done when* every claim planned for the site is listed beside the code, test or document that backs it, and anything unbacked is cut. *Positioning (1.4):* the site leads with the narrow position in landscape.md (agent and release jobs: what each can reach, whether its controls are real, with evidence). It says what is built and what is planned, never says OBAAF is the only tool that asks what an agent can reach, and names the tools OBAAF recommends for everything else. *Reserved for a ChatGPT session (decision F-62):* the table is a page of its own, `docs/site-truth-table.md`. Each row is one claim, where it would stand on the site, the file and line or the test that backs it, and "cut" where nothing does. The session changes nothing under `site/`.

The original specification follows. Leads with what OBAAF does and the fastest way to try it: a real terminal session and a real finding with its fix. Original inline SVG for the three ideas and for how check, audit and enforcement fit together; animation only where it explains, honouring `prefers-reduced-motion`. *Reserved for a Grok session (decision F-112); handed over as #146.* It changes `site/index.html`, `site/styles.css` and `site/main.js`, and nothing else (decision F-120).

The blocking one: quantizing to 64 colours had removed the accent colour from 20 of the handover's 24 screenshots, so they showed colours the pages do not have. It reproduced the handover's other measurements (keyboard stops 30, 11, 11, 13, 12 and 13, the text with JavaScript off, the request lists, contrast, no `@font-face`), checked the audit sentence against `src/obaaf/audit/` and a recorded snapshot (14 "cannot tell", exit 0, and exit 1 with the flag), and found the other five pages pixel-identical to `flagship` apart from the current-page underline. [Comment on #185](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/185#issuecomment-6901803318). *Run:* 1713 tests on the reviewed head and 1797 after merging `flagship` (Python 3.13); `obaaf catalog`, `obaaf docs build --check`, `obaaf check . --fail-on medium` and `python site/build.py` exit 0; headless Chromium 153 on the built site: the six pages at 320, 375, 641, 800, 1024, 1280, 1440 and 1920 px, light and dark, JavaScript on and off (192 loads: no page wider than the viewport, no console error, no failed request or request off the local host, no animation, lowest contrast 6.01:1 light and 6.70:1 dark), and the four format pages and the questionnaire at 320, 375, 800 and 1280 px, light and dark (none wider than the viewport); CI green on `f3973d7` ([run 43764284806](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43764284806)) and on the merged head `eabcf5a` ([run 43765175121](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43765175121)). *Not run:* Safari, Firefox, a physical phone, a screen reader, the deployed site (it deploys only from `main`); the handover's own Chromium 159 runs could not be repeated here. *Found:* #186, the questionnaire's date field has no keyboard focus outline (its styles are in the form template, outside 4.4's files). *Done when* it has been checked at desktop and phone widths, in light and dark, with JavaScript off, with no third-party fonts, scripts or trackers, and the screenshots are in the pull request. *Reserved for a Grok session (decision F-119).* It may change `site/index.html`, `site/quickstart.html`, `site/rules.html`, `site/comparison.html`, `site/case-study.html`, `site/papers.html`, `site/styles.css` and `site/main.js` to fix what the check finds (among them the home page's links to the five pages, which 4.3 noted), and it adds its screenshots as PNG files under `docs/screenshots/4.4/`; it changes nothing else.
- ☑ **4.5 `site/README.md` describes the site as it is** ([#173](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/issues/173), found by the review of 4.3). Merged as [#175](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/175) by run `phalarope` on 2026-11-15 (decision F-147). *What changed:* the README described the site before 4.2 and 4.3 (no custom domain, a domain not yet acquired, a builder that copies only the home page, a Netflix demonstration). It now says what the site holds (the home page, which does not link the five pages yet, the five pages, the questionnaire, the format pages), which pages read without JavaScript (all but the questionnaire), how to preview with the checkout installed as CI installs it, what the builder writes (only the predicate types' schemas; the settings snapshot's is not published) and that it does not empty its directory, how deployment works (the tested-revision check after CI, none on a manual dispatch, the environment's branch rule as an unread setting), and the canonical host `www.obaaf.com` beside GitHub Pages' own address, with whether the domain is set up for Pages marked as not checked. *Review:* an Opus 5.5 sub-agent, two passes (4 blocking, then none and three wording notes, applied). *Run:* 1604 tests; `obaaf docs build --check`, `python site/build.py`; CI green on `f626a54` ([run 43677086783](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43677086783)). *Not run:* a DNS lookup, the Pages API. *Found:* #176 (the settings snapshot's schema is not published at its `$id`, against D-007; the questionnaire has no canonical link and no text without JavaScript). *Done when* `site/README.md` names the canonical host as D-007 has it, says what is known about where the site is served, and lists what `site/build.py` copies and writes, every sentence checked against the build and the workflows. It changes `site/README.md` only.

Merged as [#177](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/177) by run `phalarope` on 2026-11-15 (decision F-148). *What was built:* `site/build.py` publishes every JSON Schema OBAAF ships at its `$id` (`write_schemas`; the settings snapshot's was missing, against D-007), and stops the build on an `$id` not under `https://www.obaaf.com/schemas/`, a resolved path that is not a `.json` file under `schemas/`, a duplicate `$id`, or a file that is not a JSON object. The built `assessment.html` gets a canonical link and, just before the element the form's script fills, a `<noscript>` note pointing to `spec/self-assessment.md` and `obaaf assess` (`assessment_page`; each replacement must match once or the build stops); the form template, and so the offline form, is unchanged. `site/README.md` and the changelog say so; the README no longer says the repository remains a draft. It changes `site/build.py`, its test, `site/README.md` and `CHANGELOG.md`.

- ☑ **2d.11 Agent action identification** (core; #236, closed). Done when an approval-only action and a reusable workflow in a similarly named repository do not acquire agent findings or agent attribution, and documented agent actions still do. Merged as [#268](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/268) by run `nuthatch` on 2026-11-19 (merge `731f079`; decisions F-285 to F-288; D-096 in `DECISIONS.md`, proposed). *What was built:* `AgentActions`, `action_name`, `names_action` and `leaves_repository` in `checker/workflow.py`, used by `Job.runs_agent`, `WorkflowRules._agent_step` and `_tool_action` (OBAAF-GHA-026), the local composite action reader and `agent_tools.named_agents`. A called workflow is under an agent action only when its `owner/repo` and folders are, with a `/` after the name (`anthropics/claude-code-action-docs/...` is no longer an agent's; a whole-repository action's workflows still are, a folder action's larger repository's are not). New catalogue field `not_agents` (action, `says`, `source` in the action's folder at a commit, `checked`, `read`), validated by `obaaf catalog` and built into `agent_not_actions`; one item, `anthropics/claude-code-action/agent-approval-check` at `f431ada`, taken out exactly as written and at every ref. From the review: a step whose `uses:` leaves its repository (local `..` out of the workspace, a link out of the repository anywhere on a local action's path, `..` to or above another repository's `owner/repo`) is an agent's action in any job (F-286); `agents.actions` entries are read without space, `@ref` or a trailing `/`, a local `./` entry still names a called local workflow by its first characters, and `agents.actions`/`agents.commands` must be lists of text, with entries that name no action and patterns that do not compile refused (F-287). Docs: a new section of `docs/checker-configuration.md` with the survey of every listed action's folders, four rows of `docs/upgrading.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`. *Run:* full suite 2,546 OK on `bd93adc`; `obaaf catalog`, `obaaf docs build --check`, `site/build.py` exit 0; CI 11 of 11 green on `bd93adc` ([run 44561475096](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/44561475096)), red on `bdeb282` on the three tests F-286 changed; this repository and Expectrail at `8c2f9a2` (read-only copy without `fresh-*` or `bugs/fresh`, its CI command): text, JSON and `--lanes` byte-identical to `602d941`. *Review:* one Opus 5.5 sub-agent that wrote none of it, five passes (3, 2, 1, 1, 0 blocking). *Not run:* GitHub itself on these forms (whether a runner resolves `..` in a remote action's path or follows a link out of the checkout); `coderabbitai/ai-pr-reviewer` (sign-in asked). *Left:* backslash, empty-part and leading-space forms of a step's action (#245, older than this slice); `sst/opencode`'s and `cline/cline`'s build-step actions stay the agent (only their action files were read); a `run:` step that copies an agent's action into the workspace for a later `uses: ./...`; an `agents.actions` entry of `.` matched every local call before and now none.
