### Handed over: pieces the scheduled runs cannot do

Each needs PyPI, which the scheduled runs' sessions cannot reach. They are reserved for the ChatGPT sessions (decisions F-62 and F-140) and none changes the checker's shared code.

- ☑ **H1 Dependency lock.** Merged as [#113](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/113) on 2026-11-13, the work of a ChatGPT session, reviewed and merged by run `redshank` as `review-gpt`. *What changed:* `actions/setup-obaaf/constraints.txt` pins setuptools 84.2.1 and cryptography 50.0.2 (PyYAML stays 6.0.3); `requirements.txt` was regenerated by the script and re-renders byte for byte; `pyproject.toml`'s `keys` extra needs cryptography 46.0.5 or later (CVE-2026-26007, GHSA-r6ph-v2qm-q3c2, which names `load_pem_public_key`). The changelog entry is the reviewer's (section "Dependencies"), reworded because the handover's sentence said OBAAF loads binary-curve keys. *Review:* the run and an Opus 5.5 sub-agent that wrote none of it; two blocking findings, both text (the changelog sentence, and "GitHub lists no advisories for PyYAML", which is false: GHSA-8q59-q68h-6hv4); the corrections are in [a comment on #113](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/113#issuecomment-6845837907), the description is left as it arrived. *Run:* 1011 tests on the branch merged with `flagship` (Python 3.13, locally); nine checks green on the head `a122a9c` ([run 43378120430](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43378120430)), among them the lock check against PyPI. *Not run:* cryptography 46.0.5 itself (CI installs 50.0.2), anything needing PyPI from the session. *Found by the review in `src/obaaf/verify/dsse.py`, outside the three files:* a binary-curve key ends in a traceback with cryptography 47 or later, and any elliptic curve is accepted though P-256 and P-384 are documented; slice 2d.6. The original specification follows. The three Dependabot pull requests (#40 cryptography, #42 setuptools, #43 PyYAML; see #97) cannot be merged as they are, and the hash lock can only be written with PyPI in reach. *Done when* `actions/setup-obaaf/constraints.txt` holds the versions the three ask for or newer, each chosen with its reason; `actions/setup-obaaf/requirements.txt` is written by `python scripts/lock_setup_obaaf.py` and never by hand, and `python scripts/lock_setup_obaaf.py --check` passes; a floor in `pyproject.toml` rises only where the older versions have a published vulnerability that touches what OBAAF uses, with the advisory named, and otherwise stays; the full suite passes on Python 3.10 and on 3.13, or the description says which was not run; and the description says which Dependabot pull requests it replaces, for a maintainer to close. It changes those three files and nothing else.
- ☑ **H2 The comparison tools no session could install.** Merged as [#122](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/122) on 2026-11-13, the work of a ChatGPT session published by a Claude session (run `whimbrel`'s session), reviewed and merged by run `turnstone` as `review-gpt`. *What it added to [landscape.md](landscape.md):* a dated section "H2: measured runs of Checkov, Semgrep and ci-agent-audit": Checkov 3.4.0, Semgrep 1.185.0 (rules at `a5ad1714`) and ci-agent-audit 0.1.4 (the published wheel, which differs from the source read in 1.3), with OBAAF at `c04d811`, on copies of OBAAF `461090e`, Expectrail `0509d73` and the 15 risky fixtures: counts by rule, exits, the files each tool did not read (Checkov's schema refused 5; Semgrep reported errors on 3 fixtures and 1 Expectrail file), per-fixture results, and what each reports that OBAAF does not and the reverse; the sentences that said these tools were not run are updated. KICS (no Docker), Legitify and Gato-X (tokens) stay not run. *What the review established:* OBAAF re-run at `c04d811` on targets rebuilt from git gives the page's 0, 40 and 51 findings and every per-rule count; the facts the page states about the files hold (counted by this run and an Opus 5.5 sub-agent). The third-party counts rest on the ChatGPT session's runs and the publishing session's recount: PyPI is not reachable from the reviewing session, and the page now says so. *Corrected by the review* (commit `303e40b`, wording only; [comment on #122](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/122#issuecomment-6859737866)): the stated reason Checkov refused three Expectrail workflows (the runner it named is used by all 27; the page names what only those three have); the bullet "observed none of those four rule IDs", which said more than the runs can; a heading contradicted by the page's own table; the R/D legend and five rows of the rule-by-rule table; coverage marks, attribution, and that the raw output is not published. CI green on `303e40b` ([run 43469671444](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43469671444)). *Left for later, outside H2:* statements in [landscape.md](landscape.md) made stale by other slices (the positioning rows on network restriction, escape tests and settings, "Today it reads files in the repository only", part 2 on Harden-Runner clearing GHA-020, "three checks ci-agent-audit has"); run `whimbrel` holds 2c.3, which changes the egress sentences. *From the plan (the specification it was built to):* [landscape.md](landscape.md) says Checkov, Semgrep and ci-agent-audit were not run, because the sessions could not install them. *Done when* each of the three is run at a version that is written down, on the targets and commits the page already names, on a copy of each target and with no token; the commands, the versions, the counts by rule, and what each tool reports that OBAAF does not and the reverse are written into the page in the form its parts 1 and 2 use; and every sentence of the page that calls one of them "not run" says what is now true. Legitify and Gato-X stay not run: they need a token, and this work creates none. KICS only if Docker is there. A tool that will not install is recorded as that, with the error. It changes `docs/landscape.md` and nothing else, and it does not change the positioning section's conclusions: where a result argues against one, the description says so for the reviewer.
- ☑ **H3 An official pre-commit hook** ([#151](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/issues/151), found in the review of 3.4). Merged as [#171](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/171) on 2026-11-15, the work of a ChatGPT session published from Sean's session, reviewed, corrected and merged by run `garganey` as `review-gpt`; decision D-063 in `DECISIONS.md`, accepted by Sean (F-141). *What changed:* [ci-integration.md](ci-integration.md#a-hook-before-each-commit) installs the local hook's checker in a dedicated environment from the CI example's full commit, with setuptools and the 10 dependencies from that commit's SHA-256 lock and OBAAF built with `--no-deps --no-build-isolation` (the three commands of `actions/setup-obaaf`), puts only `obaaf` and `pre-commit` on the `PATH` through a directory of links, and says what this pins and what it does not; OBAAF ships no `.pre-commit-hooks.yaml`. *Review:* an Opus 5.5 sub-agent that wrote none of it, three passes (3, 1 and 0 blocking findings). Fixed by the review: the five trials were stated as fact and credited to nobody, and "the difference was how the checker reached the committer's machine" was untrue (the remote Python hooks ran OBAAF at `d3e7c1b`, the system hooks at `a620b95`); the `PATH` line put the checker environment's `python` and `pip` first; "What was run" and "What is still missing" were stale; D-063's draft reason said an unhashed install would have been OBAAF's only one. Added by the review outside H3's file (F-142): the pin test now covers the hook's checkout and tools directory, a changelog entry, D-063. [Comment on #171](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/171#issuecomment-6886563083). *Run:* 1592 tests on each head (Python 3.13); `obaaf catalog`, `obaaf docs build --check`, `obaaf check . --fail-on medium`, `python site/build.py` exit 0; the lock at `a620b95` counted (11 packages) and its commands compared with `action.yml`; the clone, detached checkout, a build with `--no-deps --no-build-isolation` from the machine's packages, and the links, run offline; CI green on the merged head `b6dbaf5` ([run 43657970209](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43657970209)). *Not run or not verifiable:* anything needing PyPI (the hash-checked install, pre-commit, `rpds-py==2026.9.1`, the wrong-hash rejection), the final block from start to end, macOS, and each option's adoption lines, which are in the handover's evidence and not in the repository. The original specification follows. *Reserved for a ChatGPT session (decision F-140).* *Done when* three ways of offering OBAAF to pre-commit have each been tried with pre-commit itself, from a clean environment, on a clean and a risky fixture: (a) a `.pre-commit-hooks.yaml` hook with `language: python`, whose dependencies pre-commit resolves when it installs the hook; (b) the same with `additional_dependencies` pinned to the exact versions in `actions/setup-obaaf/constraints.txt`; and (c) no hook definition, only the local hook in `docs/ci-integration.md`. For each it records what was installed and at which versions, whether any hash was checked, what holds the hook's own code to a commit, and what a team writes to adopt it. The handover recommends one, with its reason weighed against control R6 (`actions/setup-obaaf` installs every dependency by exact version and SHA-256), and ships that option; `docs/ci-integration.md` says plainly what the shipped path does and does not pin. It is merged only after Sean has chosen the option and the choice is recorded in this file. It changes `.pre-commit-hooks.yaml` (new, only if the chosen option has one), `docs/ci-integration.md` and, if a hook file ships, a new `tests/test_pre_commit_hooks.py` that checks it offline; nothing else. *Handed over as [#171](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/171). Sean chose C-lock on 2026-11-14 (decision F-141).*

## Decisions

| # | Date | Decision | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| F-01 | 2026-11-11 | Agent comments end with the "Generated by Claude Code" footer. Later runs treat any comment with that footer as an agent report, not as direction. | The agent has no GitHub account of its own, so its comments appear under Sean's. Without a marker a later run could mistake its own earlier report for an instruction from Sean. |
| F-02 | 2026-11-11 | A false negative in a rule is published only together with its fix. | SECURITY.md counts false negatives as vulnerabilities and asks that details stay out of public issues. Pull requests #48 and #50 already fixed such gaps in the open, fix and description together. |
| F-03 | 2026-11-11 | Flagship decisions are logged here. A decision that changes tool behaviour or a file format also gets a *Proposed* entry in DECISIONS.md in the slice that implements it. | GOVERNANCE.md makes the maintainers the ones who accept decisions. |
| F-04 | 2026-11-11 | Settings rules get new ids, `OBAAF-SET-nnn`. No existing id changes meaning. | CONTRIBUTING.md: rule ids are stable (NFR7). |
| F-05 | 2026-11-11 | Agent commits are authored as the session's identity (Claude) with a co-author trailer and carry no `Signed-off-by`. | CONTRIBUTING.md asks for a DCO sign-off, which is a person's statement. Earlier agent-assisted commits were signed off in Sean's name with him present. Sean is not watching these runs, so the agent does not sign for him. See "Waiting on Sean". |
| F-06 | 2026-11-11 | Slice 2d.1 was taken before item 1. | The brief says not to defer a small worthwhile fix, and a false negative is a vulnerability under SECURITY.md. Everything else follows the order of the plan. |
| F-07 | 2026-11-11 | The Dependabot pull requests are reported, not touched. | They target `main`, which only a maintainer merges, and two of the three should not merge as they are. |
| F-08 | 2026-11-11 | Slice branches are named `flagship-slice/<short-name>`, not `flagship/<short-name>`. | Git cannot hold a branch `flagship` and a branch `flagship/x` at once: the first is a file where the second needs a directory. |
| F-09 | 2026-11-11 | Updates to this file and merges of `main` into `flagship` are committed straight to `flagship`. | They are bookkeeping the brief asks for at the end of every run; a pull request and review for each would add nothing. Code, tests, rule data and every other document go through a slice. |
| F-10 | 2026-11-11 | The session subscribes to pull request events only while a run is working, and unsubscribes before "Run finished". | Between runs the schedule is the only thing that should act, so two runs never work at once. The next run picks up comments, failing checks and merges in its first steps. |
| F-11 | 2026-11-11 | Slice pull requests are opened as drafts and marked ready only when the review is done and CI is green. The standing pull request into `main` is a draft whenever CI has not yet passed on its head. If a slice is merged before its review has finished anyway, the review still runs to the end and its fixes land as a follow-up slice. | In run 1 Sean merged all three pull requests within fifteen minutes of their being opened: #53 before its review had finished, #54 before any CI job had started. A maintainer may merge whenever they choose; a draft makes "not ready" visible and cannot be merged by one tap. |

## Waiting on Sean

The items that still need an action and have an issue are in #98 (items 8 to 10 and 13). Items 18 to 25 have none; they are answered on the tracking issue or in Sean's session. #94, #95, #96 and #97 are closed: the release, decision F-21, the labels and the scheduled task's prompt, and the Dependabot pull requests are settled. This list stays the full record.

1. **Nothing is left of the release** (#94, closed). Release 0.3.0 was uploaded to PyPI on 2026-11-12 at 13:25 UTC (run [43163897279](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/actions/runs/43163897279)) and its GitHub release published by Sean at 17:09 UTC. Sean said yes to a development version number, #102 set it to `0.4.0.dev0` (decision F-44), and Sean merged the standing pull request #101 at 19:36 UTC, which put it on `main`. Since then the self-assessment form on the website names the development version, beside a front page that says "Version 0.3" (see "From #102" under item 4). One thing not checked by anyone the agents know of: the line `gh attestation verify obaaf-0.3.0-py3-none-any.whl --repo Obaaf/OBAAF` in the release notes was written by an agent that could not run it.
2. **Leave draft slice pull requests for the agent to finish.** A slice is a draft until it is reviewed and green (decision F-11). Sean merged #52 to #55 himself; #56 was left as a draft and the agent merged it.

4. **PyPI is optional now.** Sean chose on 2026-11-11 to proceed without it (decision F-15). If it is ever wanted: the setting is on the scheduled task's cloud environment. Edit the task, open the environment's settings and set **Network access** to Trusted, or to Custom with "Also include default list of common package managers" ticked; it applies from the next run.

They were superseded by #113 (slice H1, merged into `flagship` on 2026-11-13): the pins they ask for are in the pipeline lock, the cryptography floor rose only to 46.0.5 (the advisory's fix, not 50.0.2 as #40 asks), and the setuptools and PyYAML floors stay (#42 and #43 ask to raise them; neither has a published vulnerability that reaches OBAAF). #43's branch was not to be merged in any case. The agents had left #97 open for him (decision F-67).
6. **Findings other tools reported on OBAAF's own repository** (zizmor 7, Octoscan 4; listed in [landscape.md](landscape.md)) are not triaged. They are queued under slice 2d.4. *Done on 2026-11-18 by slice 2d.4c (#233): see [the triage](landscape.md#triage-2026-11-18) and item 67.*

New agent workflows are pointed at GitHub Agentic Workflows, and OBAAF's agent templates stay as an example; see the [positioning](landscape.md#positioning-where-obaaf-stands-and-what-it-plans-to-do-about-it). Sean's words: "I confirm, especially if it makes onboarding easier."
8. **If you want the comparison: run ci-agent-audit** (`pip install ci-agent-audit==0.1.4`, then `ci-agent-audit scan .`) on Expectrail and on OBAAF's `tests/fixtures/workflows/risky/`, and paste the counts on the tracking issue. The agent's session was not allowed to run it (slice 1.3).
9. **A draft commit said more than it should.** Commit `ab4a177` on the branch `flagship-slice/positioning` (pull request #60) describes a gap in the checker before its fix exists, against decision F-02. The merged text does not, and #60 was squashed so the commit is not in `flagship`. The agent does not rewrite history; delete that branch if you want the commit out of the branch list (it stays reachable from the closed pull request). The gap changes which rules a job gets, not whether it is checked; it was fixed in slice 2d.2a ([#62](https://github.com/Obaaf/OBAAF/pull/62)), so the commit no longer says anything that is not public with its fix.

Since slice 2a.4 (not released) it is a finding, `OBAAF-CFG-001`, high, and no longer a note; see item 18 for what that means when Expectrail moves its pin. The entry turned out to be deliberate: Expectrail's own audit script reads `probe` as a lane of its own. Nothing has been changed in Expectrail.

Sean deleted it on 2026-11-12; an agent then listed the run's artifacts and found none. Later runs do not keep that recording.

He chose to leave the scheduled task's prompt as it is (decision F-53): a comment on a slice's issue is still information and not direction, and an issue's Priority still does not change the order.

No such token has been used, so classic branch protection, secret names, Actions permissions and policies are tested only against answers written by hand, and three readings are taken on trust ([the guide lists them](settings-audit.md#things-the-audit-takes-on-trust)). The agents do not create tokens. What it means in practice has not changed: a gate that used a v0.1 baseline or an owner-less ignore entry goes red on upgrade past 0.3.0 until `obaaf baseline migrate` is run and each ignore entry has an `owner:` line (item 15 for Expectrail).
15. **When Expectrail moves its OBAAF pin past slice 2a.1, its gate goes red until two things are done there**, both yours under its `AGENTS.md` because they touch the baseline: `obaaf baseline migrate .github/obaaf-baseline.json --owner … --reason … --expires …` (38 findings carried over on a copy of its files at `16edb91`), and an `owner:` line on the one entry under `ignore:` in `.github/obaaf.yml`. Nothing has been changed in Expectrail, and its pin (`d014b63`) is not affected until it moves.
16. **What the release's agent check does and does not show for this repository** (decision F-42). `obaaf verify release` passed its agent-history check for 0.3.0 with 0 agent commits out of 175. It counts commits that came through OBAAF's own agent lane. This repository has no such lane in use: the flagship agents commit and merge under Sean's account, with a `Co-Authored-By: Claude` line (91 commits so far). So the check is true and says nothing about them. The choice was put to Sean on 2026-11-12: leave it and keep the documents plain about it (they are, as of #100), or have the release check also count and show the commits that declare an AI co-author, without failing on them. **His answer: leave it for now** (decision F-51). No slice is added.
17. **Done: decision D-043 is accepted** (#106; decision F-54). **Still optional, and it would settle something nobody has run:** upload one SARIF report of `obaaf check` to code scanning on a scratch repository and say on the tracking issue what an accepted finding looks like there. Everything the documents say about code scanning comes from GitHub's page on its SARIF support, read on 2026-11-12; the agents cannot upload.
18. **Done: decision D-044 is accepted** (2026-11-13, #130; decision F-91) (slice 2a.4, #109; plan decisions F-55 and F-56). It was accepted with the two choices made in it. (a) A lane declaration that does nothing is a **high** finding, so it fails the default gate and a gate at `--fail-on high`; medium would be the catalogue's own word for a missing safeguard and would let a `--fail-on high` gate pass it. (b) It can be accepted **in a baseline only**: an `ignore` entry that names the rule is a configuration error, because one such entry would accept every declaration in the file. The cost of (b) is that a repository with no baseline must start one to accept such a finding. **And one thing only you can decide:** Expectrail gives a job the lane `probe` on purpose, as a label its own audit script reads. When Expectrail moves its pin past 2a.4 that is one high finding. Either accept it in Expectrail's baseline with a reason, or change the entry to `trusted` and keep the list of probes in the script; both are yours under its `AGENTS.md`. If you would rather OBAAF let an adopter put a label of its own on a job, say so here: nothing of the kind is planned.
19. **Done: decision D-046 is accepted** (2026-11-13, #130; decision F-91). **Know that a daily job came with #112** (slice 2b.3; plan decision F-65). D-046: drift is any difference from a committed snapshot, tighter or looser, with no severity and no acceptance; what one side read and the other did not fails. Since #114 put it on `main`, `.github/workflows/settings-drift.yml` runs every day at 06:23 UTC and fails if this repository's settings differ from `.github/obaaf-settings.json`. If you change a setting on purpose, commit the snapshot that run keeps as its artifact `settings-drift`; if you would rather not have the job, delete the file. Its snapshot was read with the workflow's own token, so it watches the repository, rulesets, the rules on `main`, the public view of `main`, the environments and the collaborator count, and not the classic protection on `main`.
20. **Done: decision D-045 is accepted** (2026-11-13, #130; decision F-91). **Done: the setting of this repository** (slice 2b.2, #111; plan decisions F-68 to F-70, F-96). D-045: fifteen settings rules, a finding or "cannot tell" and never a pass, exit 1 on a finding at `medium` or above by default, no acceptance for a settings finding. Choices made for you: `--fail-on medium` as the default (as `obaaf check`); "cannot tell" fails only with `--fail-on-cannot-tell`; no baseline or `ignore` for the audit, so a finding you decide to keep can only be kept out of the exit status by a higher `--fail-on`. **The setting:** the audit's one finding on this repository was real: `ci.yml#dependency-lock` runs on every pull request into `main` and is not among its eight required checks. Sean chose on 2026-11-13 to keep it advisory, and #132 gave the job `continue-on-error: true`, so the audit no longer reports it (decision F-96). The required `test` and `setup-action` jobs still fail on a lock whose versions differ from `constraints.txt` or whose hashes do not install; only the comparison with PyPI is advisory.
21. **New, optional: one run of the settings audit on Expectrail with a read-only token** (slice 2b.4, #117). The run on 2026-11-13 had no token: 10 of 15 settings were not read and 7 rules could not tell (see [the record](expectrail-settings-audit.md)). A fine-grained token with read access only to "Administration", "Secrets" and "Environments" on Expectrail, in your own terminal (the agents create no token): `GH_TOKEN=... obaaf audit /path/to/your/expectrail/clone --repo Expectrail/Expectrail --fail-on never`, from `main` (since #114). Paste the counts and the findings on the tracking issue (the report names secrets, never their values, and counts accounts by role, never who they are). Two things no read-only token shows, which you can read in Expectrail's settings and report: who may bypass the ruleset "Protect main", and any Actions policy. Also yours, under Expectrail's `AGENTS.md`, and nothing was changed: whether `merge-guard` and `agents-daily-offline` should be required checks, and whether `unity` should keep its open branch policy, given that one account both starts and approves its runs.
22. **Done: decision D-047 is accepted** (2026-11-13, #130; decision F-91). **Know what came with #119** (slice 2c.2; plan decisions F-75 to F-78). D-047: the egress action confines one command under a pinned AWF, narrows its writes to a scratch copy, refuses model-key names, and fails closed. Since #114 put it on `main`, `.github/workflows/egress-escape-tests.yml` runs on every change to the action and every Monday at 05:41 UTC on a hosted runner; it pulls AWF's images from `ghcr.io` and `busybox` from Docker Hub. **Done:** telling AWF's maintainers that a name refused by its proxy still leaves the runner in a DNS query; Sean reported it on 2026-11-13 (item 26, decision F-97). **One thing only you can decide:** whether the review passes on Opus 4.8 (F-77) are acceptable or 2c.2 should be reviewed again on Opus 5.5 when a session can get it.
23. **Done: decision D-048 is accepted** (2026-11-13, #130; decision F-91) (slice 2b.5, #121; plan decisions F-79 and F-80). D-048: every parameter of a ruleset's pull-request rule is read or named, and one the rules do not know, or a known one in another form, makes `OBAAF-SET-005` (and `OBAAF-SET-013` where workflows may approve) "cannot tell" whatever its value. Choices made for you: a new parameter from GitHub makes those answers "cannot tell" until a release places it; `OBAAF-SET-005` is "cannot tell" for any branch that requires a code owner's review unless the count alone is a finding; `require_extra_approval_for_unattributed_changes` is read from GitHub's pages (nothing on a ruleset counting 0, which is Expectrail's case). Not read, and worth knowing: whether Copilot may approve pull requests in your repositories, which GitHub says can count toward the required approvals.
24. **Done: decision D-049 is accepted** (2026-11-13, #130; decision F-91) (slice 2b.6, #124; plan decisions F-82 to F-84). D-049: each of the 25 rule types of a ruleset, and each field of classic protection, is read or named, and one the rules do not know makes the rules about a branch's requirements "cannot tell". Choices made for you: a new rule type or classic field from GitHub makes those answers "cannot tell" until a release places it; a repository with a merge queue, required deployments, required workflows, code scanning, code quality, coverage or licence compliance rules gets a "cannot tell" from `OBAAF-SET-006` or `007` for each, because what gives those results is not compared with the workflow files; a bypass of a ruleset whose only gate is deployments, code quality, coverage or licence compliance is now an `OBAAF-SET-004` finding; `OBAAF-SET-005` is "cannot tell" where a ruleset's `update` rule, classic push restrictions or a locked branch is in force. **Worth knowing:** the snapshot may now carry `other_fields`; a version before this one refuses such a file, and the first drift comparison after upgrading shows it as added where GitHub's answer has such a field. Nothing in this repository's or Expectrail's recorded answers changes.
25. **Done: decision D-050 is accepted** (2026-11-13, #130; decision F-91). **Still open: one thing only you can do** (slice 2c.3, #127; plan decisions F-87 to F-90). D-050: OBAAF-GHA-020 is cleared by GitHub's native egress firewall in enforce mode, or by OBAAF's egress action at a commit OBAAF lists as released, under the conditions in [the guide](checker-configuration.md#egress-controls); Harden-Runner is never better than "cannot tell". **Only you can decide:** when to list a commit of `actions/egress` as released (`egress_action_releases` in `src/obaaf/data/detection.yaml`), which is what lets the action clear the finding; it should be a commit the escape tests passed on, such as the head of #119. Until then the action is "cannot tell". The licence line `Apache-2.0 AND CC-BY-4.0 AND MPL-2.0` (F-88), which you accepted in the session, reached `main` with #114.
26. **Done: decision D-051 is accepted** (2026-11-16, #204; decision F-193). *As it stood:* confirm or overrule decision D-051, and two things only you can decide (slice 2c.4, #128; plan decisions F-92 to F-94). D-051 is Proposed in `DECISIONS.md`: the egress action gives AWF's proxy a resolver of its own that answers only listed names (and each model key's API host exactly), and a new input `model-keys` gives `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` or `OPENAI_API_KEY` to AWF's API proxy, never to the command; it amends D-047 and D-050, both accepted. **First:** when you list a released commit of the action (item 25), use one at or after `f0bfbd7`, the merge of #128, not the head of #119: the text the checker prints beside a cleared finding is true only from 2c.4 on (2c.5 will enforce that). **Second, done:** Sean reported to AWF's maintainers on 2026-11-13 that, in AWF's default mode, a name its proxy refuses still leaves the runner in a DNS query (its Squid configuration tests a `dst` rule for the API proxy before refusing unlisted names). The agent drafted the text in his session and he posted it; the agents posted nothing outside this repository (decision F-97). **Worth knowing:** with a real key, an agent can probably have the provider connect elsewhere for it (remote MCP servers, URLs to fetch), which nothing refuses; give an agent a key only where the provider may see everything the agent can read.
27. **Done: decision D-052 is accepted** (2026-11-16, #204; decision F-193). *As it stood:* confirm or overrule decision D-052, and one choice made beyond the plan's wording (slice 2c.5, #134; plan decisions F-98 to F-102). D-052 is Proposed in `DECISIONS.md`: `obaaf check` places each credential given to the egress action's step as the action reads its inputs, and a key given to AWF's API proxy in `model-keys` no longer keeps OBAAF-GHA-020 at "cannot tell"; the provider's API host is shown with the allow-list, and the report beside a cleared finding names the provider channel (remote MCP servers and URLs are not refused) and the job's log and artifacts. It amends D-050 and D-051, both written while the question was open. **The choice:** a credential passed to the command (in `env`, or written in `run`) also leaves the control verifiable, with the report naming it (F-98). The reason: the control says the network is limited, which such a credential does not change, and GitHub's native firewall has been verified since D-050 with a key in the agent's hands. The cost: the finding no longer pushes such a job towards `model-keys`. Overrule it and such a step goes back to "cannot tell". **Also:** only commits at or after `f0bfbd7` can now be listed in `egress_action_releases`; a test refuses older ones (item 26's first point is now enforced). **Worth reading:** issue #136, a question about `actions/checkout`'s persisted credentials and the action that no run has settled.
28. **Done: decision D-053 is accepted** (2026-11-16, #204; decision F-193). *As it stood:* confirm or overrule decision D-053 (slice 2c.8, #138; plan decisions F-103 to F-106). D-053 is Proposed in `DECISIONS.md`: a step before the egress action that only installs an agent's CLI globally, in a few exact forms, is not taken for running the agent; one that names the package in any other form is "cannot tell"; one that also runs it is "not met". **Worth knowing:** what earlier steps write to `GITHUB_ENV`, `GITHUB_PATH` or the home directory, and package-manager configuration in the working directory, can change what an accepted install runs and are not read; and issue #139, about the action's own steps and `BASH_ENV`, is open for whoever works on the action next.
29. **Done: decision D-054 is accepted** (2026-11-16, #204; decision F-193). *As it stood:* confirm or overrule decision D-054, and know the AWF finding is with GitHub (slice 2c.6, #135; plan decisions F-107, F-108). D-054 is Proposed in `DECISIONS.md`: the egress action makes the runner's sockets and pipes in `/tmp` unwritable to the command for the run and hands back only files and directories, and it refuses a workspace where a checkout kept its token (checkout v6's `includeIf` and `config.worktree` included). **With GitHub:** the underlying AWF weakness (a command in the sandbox reaches the runner's .NET diagnostic sockets through the mounted `/tmp`, which can dump a process's memory or load a library) was submitted to GitHub's bug bounty on 2026-11-14; HackerOne verification can take up to a week. The action's fix does not depend on GitHub's. **Worth weighing:** the branch and pull request #135 describe the AWF weakness publicly; when the standing pull request carries 2c.6 to `main` and the published site, you may want to decide whether to hold the AWF specifics until GitHub has responded. **Correction, 2026-11-17 (F-203):** the report was submitted through HackerOne on 2026-11-17, once Sean's access came through, not on 2026-11-14.

78. **New: confirm or overrule decision D-095** (slice 2d.5c2c, #266; plan decisions F-282 to F-284). D-095 is Proposed in `DECISIONS.md`: code in another language that runs an agent tool is read by its tokens. **The choices in it:** (a) `exec.exec('claude', flags)` and an argument list held in a variable are "cannot tell", where 2d.5c2a left them quiet; (b) options in literal pieces are a finding at the option's own severity, not only "cannot tell"; (c) one value in one place of a list (`["claude", "-p", prompt]`) is quiet, as `"$PROMPT"` is in bash (#254); (d) a value put into a command line string is "cannot tell" even inside the string's own quotes; (e) in cmd, `claude -p "%PROMPT%"` is "cannot tell"; (f) in PowerShell, `$env:NAME` is one word and another unquoted variable is "cannot tell". **This can turn a passing gate red** for workflows that run an agent CLI from code in another language.
79. **New: confirm or overrule decision D-096** (slice 2d.11, #268; plan decisions F-285 to F-288). D-096 is Proposed in `DECISIONS.md`. **The choices in it:** (a) the catalogue lists actions under an agent action that run no agent, with a source, and every other path stays the agent; (b) a listed path is taken out at every ref, though read at `f431ada`; (c) a step whose `uses:` leaves its repository is an agent's action in any job, which can turn a passing gate red (F-286, not verified on a runner); (d) `agents.actions` entries are read without `@ref` or a trailing `/`, a local entry keeps its old reading, and a configuration that is not a list of text, or an entry that names no action, is refused with an error (F-287). A job whose only agent was the approval check loses its agent findings, so a baseline entry for one of them no longer matches and fails a check at or above `--fail-on` until the baseline is written again.
